More specifically, we provide a necessary and sufficient criterion ensuring that they are complementary, and explain how pseudospectral phenomena account for these two angles to fail to be complementary.

</details>


### [56] [Failure of interior $L^p$ estimates for nonlocal elliptic equations with continuous kernels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33689)
*Chang Liu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The authors construct a fixed linear nonlocal elliptic operator whose coefficients depend continuously on x (with modulus of continuity (log(1/r))^{-1/2}) for which interior L^p regularity estimates—valid for translation-invariant operators—fail.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Interior L^p estimates are known to hold for translation-invariant nonlocal elliptic operators, but it is unclear whether they extend to operators whose kernels vary in x. The paper probes how much regularity in x is needed, testing whether mere continuity suffices, or whether a modulus of continuity of order (log(1/r))^{-1/2} is enough.

Method: A single fixed linear nonlocal elliptic operator L is built with a kernel that is symmetric in the increment variable, uniformly elliptic, and has standard bounds on its first two derivatives in that variable, while depending on x with a uniform modulus of continuity (log(1/r))^{-1/2}. The authors then construct smooth, bounded functions u_m giving a counterexample: the L^∞ norms and L^p(B_2) norms of Lu_m tend to zero, yet the fractional Laplacian norms ||(-Δ)^{σ/2}u_m||_{L^p(B_1)} have a positive liminf.

Result: The construction yields ||u_m||_{L^∞} + ||Lu_m||_{L^p(B_2)} → 0 while liminf ||(-Δ)^{σ/2}u_m||_{L^p(B_1)} > 0 for all 0<σ<2 and 1≤p<∞. Additionally, ||Du_m||_{L^p(B_4)} → 0 for σ>1, and for σ=1 these norms remain bounded (not converging to zero).

Conclusion: Interior L^p estimates for translation-invariant nonlocal operators do not extend to x-dependent operators under mere continuity of the kernel in x, even with a logarithmic modulus of continuity (log(1/r))^{-1/2}. Some stronger regularity in x is therefore necessary.

Abstract: Let $0<σ<2$ and $1\leq p<\infty$. We construct a fixed linear nonlocal elliptic operator $L$ and functions $u_m\in C^\infty(\mathbb R^n)\cap L^\infty(\mathbb R^n)$ such that \[
  \|u_m\|_{L^\infty(\mathbb R^n)}+\|Lu_m\|_{L^p(B_2)}\longrightarrow0,
  \qquad
  \liminf_{m\to\infty}\|(-Δ)^{σ/2}u_m\|_{L^p(B_1)}>0. \] The kernel is symmetric in the increment variable, uniformly elliptic, and satisfies the standard bounds on its first two derivatives in that variable. Its dependence on $x$ has a uniform modulus of continuity of order $(\log(1/r))^{-1/2}$ as $r\downarrow0$. Moreover, $\|Du_m\|_{L^p(B_4)}\to0$ for $σ>1$, and these norms remain bounded for $σ=1$. Thus the interior $L^p$ estimates for translation-invariant operators do not extend to this class under mere continuity in $x$.

</details>


### [57] [Finite-Time Blow-Up for Wave Equations with Nonlinear \(p\)-Laplacian Damping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33700)
*Menglan Liao,Mingxue Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves finite-time blow-up for a semilinear wave equation with linear elasticity, nonlinear p-Laplacian velocity damping, and a focusing power source in a 3D bounded domain, for initial data in the unstable potential well and with a new sufficient condition for positive-energy data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding blow-up for wave equations with both nonlinear damping and focusing sources is important; existing results often require sign conditions, W_0^{1,p} regularity, or only handle negative/nonpositive energy data.

Method: Combines a shifted displacement multiplier, an auxiliary quantity from the time integral of the spatial L^p-norm of the velocity gradient, and a weighted auxiliary functional; links the weight parameter to the initial cumulative quantity to absorb mixed terms.

Result: Finite-time blow-up in the energy norm for 2<p<r+1<=6 and data in H_0^1×L^2 in the unstable potential well below its depth; covers all nonzero nonpositive-energy states with no sign condition; for positive energy, a sufficient blow-up condition; upper bounds on maximal existence time for 2<p<=3 and p>3 without requiring W_0^{1,p} regularity.

Conclusion: The approach yields blow-up results under mild regularity and sign assumptions, extending existing theory for damped focusing wave equations.

Abstract: We consider a wave equation with linear elasticity, nonlinear \(p\)-Laplacian damping acting on the velocity, and a focusing power source in a bounded three-dimensional domain. For \(2<p<r+1\leq6\), we prove finite-time blow-up in the natural energy norm for initial data in \(H_0^1(Ω)\times L^2(Ω)\) lying in the unstable potential well below its depth. This includes every nonzero initial state with nonpositive total energy and requires no sign condition on the initial displacement--velocity pairing. For positive initial energy, we establish a sufficient condition for finite-time blow-up involving the \(L^2\) pairing of the initial velocity with a fixed approximation of the initial displacement. The proof combines a shifted displacement multiplier, an auxiliary quantity built from the time integral of the spatial \(L^p\)-norm of the velocity gradient, and a weighted auxiliary functional. Linking the weight parameter to the initial value of the cumulative quantity allows us to absorb the mixed terms in the auxiliary functional estimate. Together with the shifted displacement multiplier, this yields upper bounds for the maximal existence time in both ranges \(2<p\leq3\) and \(p>3\), without assuming \(W_0^{1,p}(Ω)\) regularity of the initial displacement.

</details>


### [58] [Dirichlet problem on locally finite graphs (revised and extended version)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33703)
*Mohammad Javaheri*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper analyzes the vertex-weighted Dirichlet problem on locally finite graphs, giving convergence rate results on finite graphs and several existence/uniqueness theorems on infinite graphs characterized by the number of ends.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand when the discrete Dirichlet problem (prescribing the Laplacian off a boundary set B and values on B) has solutions on graphs, and how many, connecting this to random walk behavior and a discrete analogue of Li–Tam theory.

Method: On finite graphs, the solution is characterized as the limit of an averaging process with explicit geometric rate from the spectrum of the random walk, as the limit of the heat flow, and as an energy minimizer. On infinite graphs, existence is proved under (i) a finite-width condition with summable data (no degree assumption) and (ii) bounded data with bounded degree. Ends of G relative to B are studied via harmonic functions vanishing on B.

Result: Without ends there is exactly one solution; with l ends the solution set (when nonempty) has dimension at least l, each end carrying a positive harmonic function vanishing on B. This function is bounded exactly on non-parabolic ends and necessarily unbounded on parabolic ones, a discrete analogue of a Li–Tam theorem. Hence bounded solutions are unique iff every end is parabolic.

Conclusion: The vertex-weighted Dirichlet problem is governed by the geometry of ends relative to B: uniqueness and the structure of the solution space (dimension, boundedness) are characterized by parabolicity of ends, mirroring classical Li–Tam results in the discrete setting.

Abstract: We study existence and uniqueness of solutions to the vertex-weighted Dirichlet problem on locally finite graphs. Let $B$ be a subset of the vertices of a connected graph $G$. The Dirichlet problem asks for a function whose discrete Laplacian is prescribed on $V\setminus B$ and whose values are prescribed on $B$. Each infinite connected component of $G\setminus B$ is called an end of $G$ relative to $B$. On finite graphs we show that the solution is the limit of an averaging process, with an explicit geometric rate given by the spectrum of the random walk, and is also the limit of the heat flow and the minimizer of an energy functional. On infinite graphs we prove two existence theorems: one with no assumption on vertex degrees, under a finite-width condition and summable data, and one for bounded data on graphs of bounded degree, where the solution is also unique among bounded functions. If there are no ends, the Dirichlet problem has exactly one solution. If there are $l$ ends, the solution set, when nonempty, has dimension at least $l$; each end carries a positive harmonic function vanishing on $B$. Calling an end parabolic when the random walk started in it hits $B$ almost surely, we show that this function can be taken bounded exactly on non-parabolic ends and is necessarily unbounded on parabolic ones, a discrete analogue of a theorem of Li and Tam. Consequently, bounded solutions are unique if and only if every end is parabolic.

</details>


### [59] [A Necessary and Sufficient Condition for the Existence of Global Smooth Solutions to the One-Dimensional Inviscid Burgers-Vlasov System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33714)
*Chengfeng Ren,Huimin Yu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes a sharp necessary and sufficient threshold for global smooth solvability of the 1D inviscid Burgers-Vlasov system, given by the sum of initial velocity gradient and particle density.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The challenge is that global smooth solvability of the 1D inviscid Burgers-Vlasov system involves competing effects—nonlinear fluid steepening, drag coupling, particle velocity support, and derivative hierarchies—that must be controlled simultaneously.

Method: The authors introduce a combined gradient-density quantity evolving along fluid characteristics, establish its sign invariance, use characteristic support estimates and fixed-order derivative bounds, and apply a Riccati comparison for the blow-up condition. Nonnegative regularization and compactness extend the result to finite-regularity data.

Result: Global continuation holds whenever the initial threshold (velocity gradient plus particle density) is nonnegative; if it fails at a point, an explicit finite upper bound on the maximal smooth lifespan is obtained, with negative velocity-gradient blow-up when attained.

Conclusion: The paper provides sharp necessary and sufficient conditions for global smooth solvability, valid also for initial data of any fixed finite regularity.

Abstract: We investigate global smooth solvability for the one-dimensional inviscid Burgers-Vlasov system. For a smooth initial fluid velocity and a nonnegative, compactly supported particle distribution, we establish a sharp necessary and sufficient threshold expressed through the sum of the initial velocity gradient and the initial particle density. The principal difficulty is to control simultaneously the nonlinear steepening of the fluid velocity, the drag coupling, the particle velocity support, and the full hierarchy of derivatives. To overcome it, we introduce a combined gradient-density quantity whose evolution closes along fluid characteristics. Its sign invariance, together with characteristic support estimates and fixed-order derivative bounds, yields global continuation whenever the initial threshold is nonnegative. If the threshold fails at one point, a Riccati comparison gives an explicit finite upper bound for the maximal smooth lifespan and identifies negative velocity-gradient blow-up when this bound is attained. We also use nonnegative regularization and compactness to extend the threshold result to initial data of any fixed finite regularity.

</details>


### [60] [Variational Structure of Energy-Balance Climate Models with Heterogeneous Insolation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33797)
*Gianmarco Del Sarto,Giuseppe Floridia*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves continuation and variational characterization of three stationary branches (two strict local minimizers and one Morse-index-one saddle) for Sellers-type energy-balance climate models with heterogeneous insolation perturbations, plus a mountain-pass critical point, supported by Legendre-Galerkin numerics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Stationary Sellers-type energy-balance climate models near a regime with three constant equilibria (cold, intermediate, warm) are poorly understood under heterogeneous (non-constant) insolation; the goal is to rigorously establish how these three equilibria persist and how they are characterized variationally.

Method: Perturbative and variational analysis of stationary Sellers models with insolation Q_ε = Q_* + εQ_1, on (-1,1) with degenerate diffusion κ(x)=1-x^2 and on S^2 with uniformly elliptic diffusion. Assuming the ε=0 homogeneous model has three constant equilibria θ_S<θ_M<θ_W satisfying non-degeneracy and spectral conditions, use implicit-function/continuation arguments to obtain three stationary branches. Establish the Palais-Smale condition and apply mountain-pass theory between the two local minima; a Legendre-Galerkin discretization numerically illustrates the 1D case.

Result: For small |ε|, the three equilibria continue to branches u_S^ε, u_M^ε, u_W^ε. The cold and warm branches are strict local minimizers of the energy functional; the intermediate branch u_M^ε is non-degenerate with Morse index one. The Palais-Smale condition holds and yields a mountain-pass critical point u_MP^ε at the minimax level between the two minima; under an additional isolation assumption on the homogeneous minimax level, u_MP^ε coincides with u_M^ε. Numerics display the three branches, their discrete Morse indices, and the energy landscape.

Conclusion: The three stationary states of Sellers-type climate models persist under small heterogeneous insolation perturbations and are variationally characterized as two local minima plus a Morse-index-one saddle connected via mountain-pass structure, with numerical confirmation in 1D.

Abstract: We analyse stationary Sellers-type energy-balance climate models with heterogeneous insolation $Q_\varepsilon = Q_*+\varepsilon Q_1$, both on the interval $(-1,1)$ with degenerate diffusion $κ(x)=1-x^2$ and on $\mathbb{S}^2$ with uniformly elliptic diffusion. Assuming that the homogeneous model corresponding to $\varepsilon = 0$ admits three constant equilibria $θ_S <θ_M < θ_W$ satisfying suitable non-degeneracy and spectral conditions, we prove that, for sufficiently small $\vert \varepsilon \vert $, these equilibria continue to three stationary branches $ u_S^\varepsilon$, $u_M^\varepsilon$, and $u_W^\varepsilon$. The cold and warm branches, $u_S^\varepsilon$ and $u_W^\varepsilon$ respectively, are strict local minimisers of the associated energy functional, while the intermediate branch $u_M^\varepsilon$ is non-degenerate and has Morse index one. We further establish the Palais-Smale condition and obtain a mountain-pass critical point $u^\varepsilon_{MP}$ at the minimax level between the two local minima. Under an additional isolation assumption for the homogeneous problem at the minimax level, this critical point coincides with $u_M^\varepsilon$. Lastly, a Legendre-Galerkin discretisation of the one-dimensional model numerically illustrates the three stationary branches, their discrete Morse indices, and the corresponding energy landscape.

</details>


### [61] [Optimal Control of the 2D Boussinesq Equations with Finite Codimension Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33828)
*Utpal Manna,Manil T. Mohan,Sangram Satpathi*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper analyzes an optimal control problem for the 2D Boussinesq equations where the state is constrained to a product of unit spheres (manifold constraints). It proves well-posedness of the constrained system, existence of an optimal control, first-order necessary optimality conditions, and Fréchet differentiability of the control-to-state map.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivated by the need to study optimal control of Boussinesq equations under nonlinear manifold (finite-codimensional) state constraints, ensuring the velocity and temperature remain on unit spheres (e.g., normalized/norm-preserving dynamics), which is not covered by standard unconstrained control theory.

Method: The RHS of velocity and temperature equations are projected onto tangent spaces of the manifolds M1 (unit velocity sphere) and M2 (unit temperature sphere), imposing finite-codimensional constraints keeping state on M=M1×M2. A Faedo–Galerkin construction yields a unique global strong solution with uniform energy bounds. Existence of an optimal triplet is proved, and first-order necessary conditions are derived via a coupled state–adjoint–control system. Fréchet differentiability of the control-to-state map is established, with the linearized state characterized as the unique weak solution of the constrained linearized system satisfying the tangency condition.

Result: Unique global strong solutions exist for external controls in L^2(0,T;H) with uniform energy bounds. An optimal triplet (y*,θ*,𝔥*) minimizing the quadratic cost exists, with y*,θ* on M and 𝔥*(t) ∈ T_{y*(t)}M1 for all t. The control-to-state map is Fréchet differentiable in the specified spaces, and its linearization satisfies the tangency condition.

Conclusion: Well-posedness and first-order optimality conditions are established for the 2D Boussinesq system under nonlinear manifold constraints, extending optimal control theory to norm-preserving constrained dynamics via tangent-space projections and adjoint analysis.

Abstract: We study an optimal control problem (OCP) governed by the two-dimensional (2D) Boussinesq equations, where the right-hand sides of the velocity and temperature equations are projected onto the corresponding tangent spaces of the manifolds $\mathcal{M}_1:=\{y\in\mathbb{H}:\|y\|_{\mathbb{H}}=1\}$ and $\mathcal{M}_2:=\{θ\in H:\|θ\|_H=1\}$, respectively. The resulting evolution is subject to finite-codimensional constraints, under which the state $(y,θ)$ remains on $\mathcal{M}:=\mathcal{M}_1\times\mathcal{M}_2$ throughout the time interval. A Faedo--Galerkin construction provides a unique, global, strong solution to the constrained Boussinesq system for external controls $\mathfrak{H}\in L^2(0,T;\mathbb{H})$, along with uniform energy bounds. In addition, we establish the existence of an optimal triplet $(y^*,θ^*,\mathfrak{H}^*)$ that minimizes the quadratic cost subject to the constrained Boussinesq system. Necessary conditions of the first order are obtained through a coupled state--adjoint--control system. The optimal state $(y^*,θ^*)$ remains on $\mathcal{M}$, while the optimal control lies in the tangent space: $\mathfrak{H}^*(t)\in T_{y^*(t)}\mathcal{M}_1$, for all $t\in[0,T]$. We also show that the map $\mathfrak{H}\mapsto(y_{\mathfrak{H}},θ_{\mathfrak{H}})$ in $(C([0,T];\mathbb{H})\cap L^2(0,T;\mathbb{V}))\times(C([0,T];H)\cap L^2(0,T;V))$ is Fréchet differentiable. The linearized state is the unique weak solution to the constrained linearized Boussinesq system and satisfies the necessary tangency condition. Hence, the well-posedness and first-order optimality conditions are established for the Boussinesq system under nonlinear manifold constraints.

</details>


### [62] [Well-posedness of the Burgers' equation on the half-line through the Cole-Hopf transformation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33886)
*Huy K. Vo,Athanassios S. Fokas*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The authors rigorously prove well-posedness of the initial-boundary value problem for Burgers' equation on the half-line in Sobolev-type spaces (u0 ∈ H̃^{s+1}_x, g0 ∈ H^{(2s+1)/4}_t, 1/2<s<3/2), validating a formal Fokas unified-transform/Cole-Hopf representation under two regimes: small data, or non-positive boundary data of arbitrary size.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The formal solution for Burgers on the half-line was derived via the Cole-Hopf transformation and Fokas unified transform only for smooth Schwartz-class data, leaving the validity of that representation in Sobolev-type spaces and general data unresolved. The paper aims to close this gap with a rigorous well-posedness theory.

Method: Prove validity of the formal representation for u0 ∈ H̃^{s+1}_x(0,∞) and g0 ∈ H^{(2s+1)/4}_t with 1/2<s<3/2. Combine linear estimates for the heat IBVP, Taylor-series-based nonlinear bounds, and algebraic properties of Sobolev spaces. Establish well-posedness under (1) small initial/boundary data and (2) non-positive boundary data of arbitrary size, using the strong minimum principle for the linear heat equation to guarantee global invertibility of the nonlinear Cole-Hopf map.

Result: Well-posedness is established in both regimes: small data (initial and boundary) and non-positive boundary data of arbitrary size. The formal Cole-Hopf/Fokas representation is shown to be valid in the stated Sobolev-type spaces.

Conclusion: The formal solution via Cole-Hopf and the Fokas unified transform extends rigorously to Sobolev-type data for 1/2<s<3/2, with well-posedness guaranteed either by smallness of data or by the sign condition g0≤0 exploiting a minimum-principle-based global invertibility argument.

Abstract: We rigorously establish the well-posedness of the initial-boundary value problem (IBVP) for the Burgers' equation on the half-line for data in Sobolev-type spaces. The formal solution to this problem was previously derived via the Cole-Hopf transformation and the Fokas unified transform method under the assumption of smooth, Schwartz-class data. In this work, we prove the validity of this formal representation for initial data $u_0\in \tilde{H}^{s+1}_x (0,\infty)$ and boundary data $g_0 \in H_t^{{(2s+1)}/{4}}$ where $1/2<s<3/2$. Well-posedness is established under two complementary conditions: (1) sufficiently small initial and boundary data, and (2) non-positive boundary data of arbitrary size. The latter condition utilizes the strong minimum principle for the corresponding linear heat equation to ensure the global invertibility of the nonlinear Cole-Hopf map. The proofs combine linear estimates for the heat IBVP with nonlinear bounds obtained through Taylor series representations and the algebraic properties of Sobolev spaces.

</details>


### [63] [Recovery of a source of anomalous diffusion from space-time sparse data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33896)
*Xing Cheng,Bangti Jin,Yavar Kian,Zhiyuan Li*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes recovery of general L^2 sources in a time-fractional sub/super-diffusive anomalous diffusion equation on the unit disk, using boundary measurements that are sparse in both space and time (two boundary points, discrete times near final time).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Inverse source problems are important, but the anomalous (fractional) diffusion setting with sparse measurements is challenging. The authors aim to show that even highly limited boundary data suffice to determine a broad class of sources.

Method: They analyze a time-fractional sub- and super-diffusive equation on the unit disk. Using the memory effect of the model, complex analysis properties, and an explicit representation of solutions with separated variables, they prove uniqueness/recovery of sources from sparse boundary observations at two chosen boundary points over a discrete set of times arbitrarily close to the final time.

Result: A general class of L^2 sources (with separated variables) is shown to be recoverable from boundary measurements that are sparse in both space and time.

Conclusion: Sparse boundary observations near the final time, combined with the memory effect of fractional diffusion, are sufficient to uniquely recover a broad class of anomalous diffusion sources.

Abstract: We study an inverse source problem for a time-fractional sub- and super-diffusive anomalous diffusion equation on the unit disk with sparse boundary observations. We prove the recovery of a general class of $L^2$ sources for anomalous diffusion, with separated variables, from boundary measurements that are sparse in both space and time, using observations at two carefully chosen boundary points on a discrete set of times arbitrarily close to the final time. The analysis relies heavily on the memory effect of the model, properties of complex analysis and the representation of solutions.

</details>


### [64] [A remark on the global minimality of the cracktip](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33900)
*Francesco Deangelis,Camillo De Lellis,Matteo Focardi*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Short proof that the planar cracktip is a global minimizer of the Mumford–Shah functional, using interior/Dirichlet boundary regularity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Provide a concise alternative proof of a known result: that the planar cracktip globally minimizes the Mumford–Shah functional, without heavy machinery.

Method: Construct a Dirichlet minimizer with nonempty discontinuity set, show that this set contains no simple closed curves, then invoke interior and Dirichlet boundary regularity to obtain an interior regular loose end whose blow-up yields the cracktip.

Result: The constructed Dirichlet minimizer's discontinuity set has no simple closed curves; regularity implies an interior regular loose end, and its blow-up recovers the cracktip as a global minimizer.

Conclusion: The planar cracktip is a global minimizer of the Mumford–Shah functional, established via a short argument relying on regularity theory.

Abstract: We give a short proof that the planar cracktip is a global minimizer of the Mumford--Shah functional, based on interior and Dirichlet boundary regularity. We construct a Dirichlet minimizer with nonempty discontinuity set and show that this set contains no simple closed curves. The regularity results then imply the existence of an interior regular loose end, at which a blow-up yields the cracktip.

</details>


### [65] [Dynamic Interpolation and Higher-Order Turbulence Scales in 3D Navier-Stokes Flows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33911)
*Moses Patson Phiri,Zoran Grujić*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper rules out a class of strongly turbulent finite-time blow-up scenarios for the 3D unforced Navier-Stokes equations by combining spatial analyticity with the geometric sparseness of intense-activity regions, showing the strong solution extends analytically past a candidate blow-up time.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The regularity/blow-up problem for the 3D Navier-Stokes equations remains open; the authors aim to exploit the interplay between analyticity radius and the sparse geometry of high-variation fluid regions to exclude strongly turbulent blow-up.

Method: Interpret higher-order derivative ratios as turbulence scales; introduce time-weighted bridge inequalities linking lower- and higher-order derivative norms; use the time weights to gain flexibility so the analyticity-radius lower bound dominates the sparseness scale of super-level sets (entering the dissipation regime); combine this scale comparison with a harmonic-measure maximum principle on one-dimensional sparse sets to obtain a contraction in the relevant derivative norm.

Result: A contraction of the derivative norm is established, which excludes a class of strongly turbulent blow-up scenarios, so the strong solution extends analytically beyond the candidate blow-up time.

Conclusion: Coupling spatial analyticity with geometric sparseness (via time-weighted bridge inequalities and a harmonic-measure maximum principle) provides a viable route to rule out strong turbulent singularity formation in 3D Navier-Stokes.

Abstract: We investigate the three-dimensional unforced Navier-Stokes equations through the interaction between spatial analyticity and the geometric sparseness of regions of intense fluid activity (i.e., regions where the fluid's motion varies sharply). Interpreting higher-order derivative ratios as turbulence scales, we introduce time-weighted bridge inequalities that quantify the relationship between lower- and higher-order derivative norms. The time weights give the derivative ratios greater flexibility while ensuring that the resulting lower bound on the radius of analyticity dominates the sparseness scale of the super-level sets, indicating that the flow has entered the dissipation regime. Combining this scale comparison with a harmonic-measure maximum principle on one-dimensional sparse sets yields a contraction in the relevant derivative norm, thereby excluding a class of strongly turbulent blow-up scenarios. Consequently, the strong solution extends analytically beyond the candidate blow-up time.

</details>


### [66] [Asymptotic stability of transonic steady nozzle flows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33951)
*Masashi Ohnawa,Masahiro Suzuki*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves nonlinear asymptotic stability of stationary solutions to quasi-1D barotropic Euler equations in a finite nozzle with variable cross-section, covering fully subsonic flows, smooth sonic transitions to supersonic, and flows with a stationary shock, with no smallness restrictions on mass flux, area variation, or exit pressure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Establish stability of all stationary nozzle flows arising from subsonic inflow, including the challenging smooth subsonic-to-supersonic transition, which previously lacked a nonlinear stability result due to sonic-point degeneracy.

Method: Couple energy structures adapted separately to subsonic and supersonic regimes; the coupling supplies dissipation to control the smooth sonic transition, and is combined with shock dynamics to yield a unified nonlinear stability framework. Geometrical assumptions: A_xx>0 at the smooth sonic transition and A_x>0 at the shock.

Result: All such stationary solutions are asymptotically stable under sufficiently small perturbations, without smallness restrictions on mass flux, |A_x|, or exit pressure, provided the stationary solution exists. This is the first nonlinear asymptotic stability result for a stationary flow passing smoothly from subsonic to supersonic in a finite nozzle.

Conclusion: A unified nonlinear stability theory is obtained for smooth and discontinuous transonic nozzle flows, overcoming the characteristic degeneracy at the sonic point via regime-adapted coupled energy estimates.

Abstract: In the present article, we prove the nonlinear asymptotic stability of stationary solutions to the quasi-one-dimensional barotropic Euler equations in a finite-length nozzle with variable cross-sectional area $A(x)$. We consider all stationary flow configurations arising from subsonic inflow: flows that remain subsonic throughout the nozzle, flows that pass continuously through a sonic state and remain supersonic up to the exit, and flows that subsequently return to the subsonic regime through a stationary shock. We prove that all these stationary solutions are asymptotically stable with respect to sufficiently small perturbations. No smallness restrictions are imposed on the mass flux, the magnitude of $A_x$, or the prescribed exit pressure, provided that the corresponding stationary solution exists. The only geometrical assumptions are that $A_{xx}>0$ at a smooth subsonic--supersonic transition, and $A_x>0$ at a stationary supersonic--subsonic shock. To the best of our knowledge, this gives the first nonlinear asymptotic stability result for a stationary flow passing smoothly from the subsonic to the supersonic regime in a finite-length nozzle. The principal analytical difficulty is the degeneracy of the characteristic structure at the sonic point. We overcome this difficulty by coupling energy structures adapted respectively to the subsonic and supersonic regimes. This coupling provides the dissipation needed to control the smooth sonic transition, and when combined with the dynamics of the shock, yields a unified nonlinear stability framework for smooth and discontinuous transonic nozzle flows.

</details>


### [67] [Global $c^{1,α}$ regularity and fine behavior at critical points for quasilinear elliptic equations with Orlicz growth and unbounded drift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33961)
*José Erivamberto Lima Oliveira*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes global boundary regularity and sharp pointwise regularity at critical points for bounded weak solutions of quasilinear elliptic Dirichlet problems in Orlicz-Sobolev spaces with possibly unbounded drift and source terms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To handle quasilinear elliptic problems featuring non-standard Lieberman-type growth, natural gradient lower-order terms, an unbounded L^q source, and an unbounded L^s spatial drift weight, overcoming analytical obstructions posed by the unbounded drift across the anisotropic growth gap.

Method: Moser iteration for global Holder continuity up to the boundary; a novel smooth regularization scheme for general homogeneous constant-coefficient operators merging Lieberman and Cianchi-Maz'ya approximation frameworks; a variational perturbation approach for global C^{1,alpha}; geometric compactness and blow-up arguments at interior and boundary critical points.

Result: Global Holder and then global C^{1,alpha} regularity up to the boundary; improved pointwise C^{1,alpha} regularity at critical points where the gradient vanishes. At boundary critical points the improvement decouples from lower-order terms and bypasses both boundary datum regularity and domain smoothness. Sharpness of the integrability threshold for the drift in the subcritical regime is also discussed.

Conclusion: The work provides a unified global regularity theory for general quasilinear Orlicz-Sobolev Dirichlet problems with unbounded drift, and shows a striking boundary critical-point phenomenon independent of data and domain regularity, along with the sharpness of the drift integrability threshold.

Abstract: We investigate the global regularity up to the boundary and the fine pointwise behavior along the critical set for bounded weak solutions to general quasilinear elliptic Dirichlet problems in Orlicz-Sobolev spaces. Our structural framework encompasses operators with non-standard Lieberman-type growth alongside lower-order terms featuring natural gradient growth, an unbounded source term in $L^q$, and an unbounded spatial drift weight in $L^s$. To overcome the analytical obstructions posed by the unbounded drift across the anisotropic growth gap, we first establish global Hölder continuity up to the boundary via Moser iteration. We then construct a novel smooth regularization scheme for general homogeneous constant-coefficient operators, merging the approximation frameworks of Lieberman and Cianchi-Maz'ya, to derive global $C^{1,α}$ regularity up to the boundary through a variational perturbation approach. Building upon this global theory, we employ a geometric compactness and blow-up argument to prove that at any interior or boundary critical point where the gradient vanishes, the solution achieves an improved pointwise $C^{1,α}$ regularity. Remarkably, at boundary critical points, this quantitative improvement decouples from the lower-order terms and completely bypasses both the boundary datum regularity and the domain smoothness. Finally, we discuss the sharpness of the integrability threshold required for the unbounded drift weight in the subcritical regime.

</details>


### [68] [Swirl ill-posedness for incompressible fluids in (sub)critical spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33973)
*Tobias Barker,Ruilin Hu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves that norm inflation occurs for the swirl component of the axisymmetric Navier-Stokes equations under small initial data in the critical space H^{1/2} (and subcritical Sobolev spaces), and for the 3D Euler swirl vector in H^3, via an anisotropic scaling of Luo's construction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Global regularity for 3D Navier-Stokes/Euler with zero swirl is known (Ladyzhenskaya; Ukhovskii-Yudovich), but whether well-posedness persists for smooth axisymmetric data with small (absolute constant) initial swirl in a critical space has remained open.

Method: The authors introduce an anisotropic scale within Luo's supercritical norm inflation construction and carry out an anisotropic bootstrap argument to control the swirl remainder term sufficiently small.

Result: Norm inflation occurs for the Navier-Stokes scalar swirl component even with small initial data in the critical space H^{1/2} or subcritical Sobolev spaces; norm inflation is also obtained for the 3D Euler swirl vector in the subcritical space H^3.

Conclusion: Small initial swirl (below an absolute constant) in a critical space does not guarantee well-posedness; norm inflation shows instability/ill-posedness for the swirl component in both Navier-Stokes and Euler settings.

Abstract: For the 3D Navier-Stokes equations with sufficiently smooth initial data having zero swirl $u_{0θ}\equiv 0$, Ladyzhenskaya \cite{L1968} and Ukhovskii and Yudovich \cite{UY1968} established global regularity. It has since remained open as to whether well-posedness still holds for smooth axisymmetric initial data, with the initial swirl being smaller than an \textit{absolute} constant in a critical space. In this remark, we prove \textit{norm inflation} occurs for the Navier-Stokes scalar swirl component, with initial smallness in the critical space $\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}$ or even subcritical Sobolev spaces. We also obtain norm inflation for the 3D Euler swirl \textit{vector} in the Euler subcritical space $\dot{H}^3$. Our results are achieved by introducing an anisotropic scale in Luo's supercritical norm inflation construction \cite{Luo24}, as well as performing an anisotropic bootstrap argument to get a sufficiently small swirl remainder term.

</details>


### [69] [Transport by rough vector fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33993)
*Finn Rudolph*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: An expository thesis presenting DiPerna–Lions theory of regular Lagrangian flows for Sobolev vector fields with bounded divergence, with detailed proofs, applications to 2D Euler, and three counterexamples, plus three technical contributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The classical Cauchy–Lipschitz theorem requires spatial Lipschitz continuity, which is too restrictive for fluid mechanics. DiPerna–Lions extended well-posedness to Sobolev fields with bounded divergence, but the theory needs a careful, self-contained exposition, and some constructions in existing counterexamples have dimensional limitations.

Method: Expository treatment of DiPerna–Lions theory via renormalized solutions of the transport equation: proves existence, uniqueness and stability of weak/renormalized solutions, deduces existence (autonomous fields) and uniqueness of regular Lagrangian flows, and discusses 2D Euler applications. Counterexamples analyzed via divergence-free fields.

Result: Establishes existence/uniqueness/stability of solutions and uniqueness of regular Lagrangian flows; demonstrates three failure modes of uniqueness (no growth condition at infinity, fractional Sobolev regularity, and L^1 solutions when ∇b ∈ L^r, r<d).

Conclusion: The thesis provides a rigorous self-contained account of DiPerna–Lions theory. Its contributions are: smooth approximations of b with uniformly bounded divergence for existence, a direct proof of uniqueness of regular Lagrangian flows from continuity-equation uniqueness, and a cube-translating auxiliary field construction valid for all d≥2.

Abstract: The Cauchy-Lipschitz theorem guarantees that the ODE $\dotγ= b(t,γ)$ is well-posed if $b$ is Lipschitz in space, an assumption too restrictive for applications such as fluid mechanics. In 1989, DiPerna and Lions extended the theory to Sobolev vector fields with bounded divergence. Well-posedness then holds not for individual trajectories but for regular Lagrangian flows: flows that solve the ODE for almost every initial point and compress volume by at most a bounded factor. Their method rests on renormalized solutions of the associated transport equation.
  This thesis is primarily expository: it presents the DiPerna-Lions theory with detailed proofs. We treat existence, uniqueness and stability of weak and renormalized solutions of the transport equation, deduce existence (for autonomous fields) and uniqueness of regular Lagrangian flows, and discuss applications to the two-dimensional Euler equations. Three counterexamples with divergence-free fields show that uniqueness can fail without a growth condition at infinity, if $b$ has only fractional Sobolev regularity (due to DiPerna and Lions), or for $L^1$ solutions when $\nabla b \in L^r$, $r<d$ (the simplest case of a recent construction by Bruè, Colombo and Kumar).
  Contributions are (1) smooth approximations of $b$ with uniformly bounded divergence for the existence proof; (2) a direct proof of the known uniqueness of regular Lagrangian flows from uniqueness for the continuity equation; (3) a construction of the auxiliary cube-translating field in the example of Bruè, Colombo and Kumar that works for all $d \ge 2$, replacing an extension from two dimensions that is not a vector field on $\mathbb R^d$ for $d \ge 3$.

</details>


### [70] [A variational model with finite carrying capacity for segregated multi-species populations in the whole Euclidean space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33996)
*Pu-Zhao Kow,Chang-Hong Wu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: A variational problem for multiple segregated species in a nonhomogeneous environment is formulated in all of R^n with a finite carrying-capacity constraint, and shown to arise as the sharp-interface limit of penalized energies with strong crowding and interspecific competition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing segregated-species models typically work in bounded domains and neglect saturation; the authors want to capture saturated regions where species reach maximal density in a nonhomogeneous, unbounded setting, and to justify the constrained energy as a genuine modeling object.

Method: Tools from potential theory, especially free boundary methods, are used to analyze the constrained variational problem set in the whole Euclidean space with a finite carrying-capacity constraint; a Gamma-convergence-type analysis connects it to penalized energies with increasingly strong crowding and interspecific competition terms.

Result: Every sequence of minimizers of the penalized energies, regardless of the relative rates of the two penalties, has a subsequence converging strongly to a minimizer of the constrained energy, so the constrained energy is the variational limit.

Conclusion: The constrained energy is a well-founded model of saturated segregated species, obtained as a robust variational limit independent of penalty rates, with free boundary methods the key technical vehicle.

Abstract: We study a variational problem motivated by models of multiple segregated species in a nonhomogeneous environment. The analysis combines tools from potential theory, in particular free boundary methods. The main novelty is that we formulate the variational problem in the whole Euclidean space while imposing a finite carrying capacity constraint, which allows us to investigate saturated regions where the species attain their maximal density. In addition, we provide a modeling interpretation of the constrained energy. We show that it arises as the variational limit of energies with increasingly strong crowding and interspecific competition penalties, in the sense that, regardless of their relative rates, every sequence of minimizers of the penalized energies admits a subsequence that converges strongly to a minimizer of the constrained energy.

</details>


### [71] [Uniqueness of radial bound states for an equation with an odd cubic-like nonlinearity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34025)
*Wenjing Chen,Zexi Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: For the odd cubic equation Δu+u(|u|-b)(c-|u|)=0 in R^n, radial bound states with k nodes exist iff 0<a=b/c<1/2; uniqueness for fixed k holds in n=2, and for explicit a-thresholds in n=3,4, with exact one-node ranges given for all n≥3.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the odd counterpart of the cubic nonlinearity in Tang's prescribed-node uniqueness conjecture, where existence and uniqueness of nodal radial solutions are not yet understood.

Method: Amplitude confinement argument; controlled extensions preserving sharp comparison estimates; subcritical truncation for existence; tangent comparison and quotient inequality for one-node exact ranges; small-a uniqueness via a massive double-power result.

Result: Existence of k-node radial bound states iff 0<a<1/2. Uniqueness per k for all 0<a<1/2 in n=2, for a≥(7-3√5)/2 in n=3, and a≥(43-5√33)/32 in n=4. One-node exact ranges computed for all n≥3; threshold is dimension-independent for 3≤n≤23, implicit for n≥24.

Conclusion: The odd cubic analog exhibits threshold-type uniqueness behavior; technical obstacles (second positive zero, no global comparison, supercritical growth) are resolved, extending prescribed-node uniqueness theory.

Abstract: We investigate radial bound states with prescribed nodes for the odd cubic equation \[
  Δu+u(\abs{u}-b)(c-\abs{u})=0
  \quad\text{in }\R^n,\qquad n\ge2,\quad 0<b<c. \] Writing $a=b/c$, we prove that a radial bound state with exactly $k\ge1$ simple zeros and positive central value exists if and only if $0<a<1/2$. For each $k$, this solution is unique for all $0<a<1/2$ in dimension two, for $a\ge(7-3\sqrt5)/2$ in dimension three, and for $a\ge(43-5\sqrt{33})/32$ in dimension four. For one node, we identify exact ranges obtained from a tangent comparison and a quotient inequality in every dimension $n\ge3$; the threshold is dimension-independent for $3\le n\le23$ and is characterized implicitly for $n\ge24$. These results address the natural odd counterpart of the cubic nonlinearity highlighted in Tang's prescribed-node uniqueness conjecture [M. Tang, \emph{Invent. Math.}, 2026]. The main challenges are the second positive zero of the cubic source, the lack of a global comparison inequality, and supercritical cubic growth in higher dimensions. We overcome them by an amplitude confinement argument, controlled extensions preserving sharp comparison estimates, and a subcritical truncation for existence. A complementary small-$a$ uniqueness range in dimensions three to five follows from a recent massive double-power result.

</details>


### [72] [A superprocess model for eDNA transport in random river environment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34048)
*Hidekazu Yoshioka,Motoh Tsujimura,Ayumi Hashiguchi*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Proposes a stochastic PDE framework for river eDNA concentration combining long-memory flow (superposition of Ornstein-Uhlenbeck processes), square-root multiplicative noise, and robust control via relative entropy to give closed-form bounds on expected eDNA concentration under model uncertainty.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: eDNA monitoring in rivers is hampered by two challenges: river flow fluctuates with long memory, making concentration predictions difficult, and eDNA dynamics models are inevitably misspecified, undermining reliability of predictions.

Method: eDNA concentration is modeled by a stochastic partial differential equation with transport, decay, source, and square-root multiplicative noise; flow velocity is a superposition of long-memory Ornstein-Uhlenbeck-type processes. A closed-form Laplace functional enables proof of existence and uniqueness in law on an unbounded domain. Model uncertainty is assessed through a relative-entropy robust control formulation yielding closed-form lower/upper bounds of expected eDNA concentration and worst-case distortions.

Result: Long memory of flow velocity induces superdiffusive eDNA transport, with variance of transported distance growing superlinearly (ensemble sense). Closed-form bounds on expected eDNA concentration and worst-case distortions are obtained. Applied to eDNA and flow velocity data for upstream migration of Plecoglossus altivelis altivelis in a Japanese river, the bounds provide a computationally inexpensive tool for quantifying the range of eDNA concentrations under model uncertainty.

Conclusion: The framework jointly handles long-memory flow and model misspecification, offering theoretically grounded, closed-form, computationally cheap bounds for eDNA concentration prediction, demonstrated on real river data.

Abstract: Environmental DNA (eDNA) is increasingly used to monitor aquatic organisms in rivers, yet predicting its concentration and assessing the reliability of such predictions remain challenging because river flow fluctuates with long memory and models of eDNA dynamics are inevitably misspecified. We propose a stochastic modeling framework for eDNA concentration in rivers that addresses both issues. The eDNA concentration is described by a stochastic partial differential equation with transport, decay, source, and square-root multiplicative noise, and the flow velocity is modeled by a superposition of Ornstein-Uhlenbeck-type processes with long memory. We show that the long memory of the flow velocity induces superdiffusive transport of eDNA, with the variance of the transported distance growing superlinearly in the ensemble sense. The proposed model admits a closed-form Laplace functional, based on which we prove the existence and uniqueness in law of the solution on an unbounded domain. We further evaluate the effects of model uncertainty through a robust control formulation based on the relative entropy, in which the lower and upper bounds of the expected eDNA concentration, together with the worst-case distortions, are obtained in closed form. The framework is applied to eDNA and flow velocity data collected for the upstream migration of Plecoglossus altivelis altivelis in a river in Japan. The results demonstrate that the closed-form bounds provide a computationally inexpensive tool for quantifying the range of eDNA concentrations that can be expected under model uncertainty.

</details>


### [73] [The Dirac Equation in the Classical Limit: A Duality Approach to a Convergence Rate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34086)
*François Golse,Jakob Möller*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes a quantitative semiclassical limit for the Dirac equation with external electromagnetic fields using a Wasserstein-1-type distance adapted to matrix-valued density operators and Wigner measures, yielding a rate of convergence that improves on prior results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Previous semiclassical-limit results for the Dirac equation did not provide quantitative convergence rates. Existing Wasserstein-type distances comparing density matrices with classical phase-space distributions were limited to the scalar case, so a suitable metric for matrix-valued objects was needed.

Method: A dual formulation based on a Wasserstein-1-type distance tailored to matrix-valued density operators and matrix-valued Wigner measures, extending earlier scalar metrics. The analysis builds on a Lagrange-multiplier formulation of the semiclassical limit for the full matrix-valued Wigner equation, without projecting onto energy eigenspaces, as introduced in prior work (Golse, Leopold, Mauser, Möller, Saffirio).

Result: A quantitative convergence rate in the semiclassical parameter is proven, improving previous results for the Dirac equation.

Conclusion: The extended Wasserstein-type dual framework provides an effective tool for quantitative semiclassical analysis of matrix-valued quantum systems, delivering improved convergence rates for the Dirac equation.

Abstract: We study the semiclassical limit of the Dirac equation with external electromagnetic fields using a dual formulation based on a Wasserstein-1-type distance. This distance is tailored to matrix-valued density operators and matrix-valued Wigner measures and extends earlier metrics comparing density matrices with classical phase-space distributions in the scalar case. We prove a quantitative convergence rate in the semiclassical parameter, improving previous results for the Dirac equation. Our approach relies on a Lagrange-multiplier formulation of the semiclassical limit for the full matrix-valued Wigner equation (without projection onto energy eigenspaces), introduced in [F. Golse, N. Leopold, N. Mauser, J. Möller, C. Saffirio, J. Math. Pures Appl., (2026)].

</details>


### [74] [A theoretically exact filtered back-propagationformula for single-time wave inversion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35302)
*Markus Haltmeier*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies single-time wave inversion—recovering initial pressure f from the wave field at one time T (key for 2D photoacoustic imaging)—and analyzes the Fourier back-propagation method's error, then derives an exact filtered back-propagation formula analogous to filtered backprojection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Single-time wave inversion is the core reconstruction step in full-field photoacoustic projection imaging in 2D. The standard back-propagation via the Fourier multiplier 2cos(cT|ξ|) is exact only in odd dimensions and fails in two, so understanding and correcting its error is essential.

Method: The authors compute the error of Fourier back-propagation in closed form with rigorous bounds for the 2D case, and construct an exact explicit filtered back-propagation formula f = 2 W_T M_K W_T f, where M_K is a filtering operator, mirroring filtered backprojection for the Radon transform.

Result: A closed-form expression and bounds for the error of the Fourier-multiplier back-propagation in two dimensions, along with an exact filtered back-propagation reconstruction.

Conclusion: The naive Fourier back-propagation is inexact in 2D, but this deficiency can be corrected by an explicit filtering step, yielding exact single-time wave inversion analogous to Radon filtered backprojection.

Abstract: We consider single-time wave inversion, that is, recovering the initial pressure $f$ from the wave field $\mathcal{W}_T f = u(\cdot,T)$ at a single time $T$, where $u$ solves the wave equation with sound speed $c$ and initial data $(f,0)$. In two dimensions this is the main reconstruction step of full-field photoacoustic projection imaging, where each camera image is a two-dimensional wave field at a single time instant. An efficient direct method is back-propagation by the Fourier multiplier $2\cos(cT|ξ|)$. It yields exact inversion in odd dimensions but not in two. We give its error in closed form, with bounds, and derive an exact explicit filtered back-propagation $f = 2\mathcal{W}_T \mathcal{M}_K \mathcal{W}_T f$ in analogy to filtered backprojection for the Radon transform.

</details>


### [75] [Positive solutions of Yamabe-type equations with general potentials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34417)
*Hongyu Chen,Li Ma*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves that on a closed Riemannian manifold of dimension n≥3, the critical Yamabe-type equation with a potential ψ has a smooth positive solution iff the linear part has positive first eigenvalue.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize solvability of a nonlinear critical (Yamabe-type) PDE with potential on closed manifolds, i.e., establish a sharp necessary and sufficient condition (positive first eigenvalue) for existence of positive solutions.

Method: For n≥6, combines an energy estimate under exact local mass constraints with an intersection argument for finite cycles of atomic measures; for 3≤n≤5, relies on the Bahri–Brezis theorem.

Result: The equation admits a smooth positive solution if and only if its linear part has positive first eigenvalue, across all dimensions n≥3.

Conclusion: Establishes a complete existence criterion for this critical equation, resolving the problem for all dimensions and highlighting distinct techniques for high versus low dimensions.

Abstract: Let $(M^n,g)$ be a smooth closed connected Riemannian manifold of dimension $n\ge3$, and let $ψ\in C^\infty(M,\mathbb R)$. We prove that the equation $$ -\frac{4(n-1)}{n-2}Δ_g u+ψu=u^{\frac{n+2}{n-2}} $$ has a smooth positive solution if and only if its linear part has positive first eigenvalue. In dimensions $n\ge6$, the proof combines an energy estimate under exact local mass constraints with an intersection argument for finite cycles of atomic measures; dimensions $3\le n\le5$ follow from the theorem of Bahri and Brezis.

</details>


### [76] [An Alt-Caffarelli problem for elastic graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34447)
*Marius Müller*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies an Alt-Caffarelli-type free boundary problem for symmetric elastic graphs with fixed boundary height, establishing a sharp threshold for the weight coefficient governing existence, classifying sign-changing minimizers as systems of three non-line Eulerian elasticae, and proving non-flatness of free boundary points.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The work is motivated by the classical Alt-Caffarelli free boundary framework, extended here to symmetric elastic graphs. The key motivating question is what role the weight coefficient in front of the measure term plays, and in particular how it determines existence, regularity, and shape of minimizers.

Method: The authors analyze an Alt-Caffarelli-type free boundary problem for symmetric elastic graphs with fixed boundary height. They study existence of minimizers, derive a sharp threshold for the weight coefficient, and provide a geometric/regularity description of minimizers via Eulerian elasticae.

Result: A sharp threshold for the weight coefficient is identified: below it existence is guaranteed. All sign-changing minimizers are shown to be described by a system of three Eulerian elasticae, none of which are lines. Additionally, free boundary points are proven to be non-flat.

Conclusion: These findings clarify the structure of minimizers and yield a better understanding of the nonexistence phenomenon that occurs above the weight threshold, connecting regularity, shape, and existence in the elastic Alt-Caffarelli setting.

Abstract: We study an Alt-Caffarelli-type free boundary problem for symmetric elastic graphs with fixed boundary height. Our results concern existence of minimizers and their description in terms of regularity and shape. It turns out that the weight coefficient in front of the measure term is decisive for existence. We identify a sharp threshold for this coefficient below which existence is guaranteed. Moreover, we find that all sign-changing minimizers are described by a system of three Eulerian elasticae, none of which are lines. Another result shows non-flatness of free boundary points, a classical question in the study of Alt-Caffarelli problems. These properties lead to a better understanding of the nonexistence phenomenon above the aforementioned weight threshold.

</details>


### [77] [Quantitative approximation theorem of neural operators for the Dirichlet-to-Neumann map](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35404)
*Pu-Yun Kow,Andrea Aspri,Elisa Francini,Jenn-Nan Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper constructs neural operator surrogates for the Dirichlet-to-Neumann (DN) map arising in Calderón's inverse conductivity problem and derives quantitative bounds on their parametric complexity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Numerical methods for reconstructing unknown conductivity from the DN map must repeatedly evaluate the DN map at given conductivity functions, which requires solving a boundary value problem and taking Neumann data—a time-consuming and ineffective procedure. A surrogate is therefore desirable to replace the forward map from conductivities to DN maps.

Method: Neural operators are constructed as surrogates for the forward DN map, with a quantitative analysis of their parametric complexity under suitable assumptions, characterizing how depth, spectral ranks, and projection ranks scale with approximation accuracy.

Result: Under suitable assumptions, the depth of the neural operators grows at most logarithmically with ε^{-1}, while the relevant spectral and projection ranks grow only algebraically.

Conclusion: General neural operators suffer from the curse of parametric dimensionality, but the constructed neural operators for the DN map avoid this curse.

Abstract: In this paper, we study neural operators for the Dirichet-to-Neumann map (DN map), which is encountered in the famous Calderón's problem. In many numerical methods used for reconstructing the unknown conductivity from the knowledge of the DN map need to evaluate the corresponding DN map at a given conductivity function, which involves solving the boundary value problem for the conductivity equation and taking the Neumann data. Such procedure is time consuming and not effective. Hence, it is a favorable strategy to construct a surrogate to replace the forward map mapping conductivity functions to corresponding DN maps. The main theme of this paper is to derive quantitative bounds on the parametric complexity of these neural operators. In particular, under suitable assumptions, their depth grows at most logarithmically with $\varepsilon^{-1}$, while the relevant spectral and projection ranks grow algebraically. It should be noted that general neural operators suffer from "the curse of parametric dimensionality". This work demonstrates the construction of neural operators for the DN map that does not suffer from such curse.

</details>


### [78] [Slow blowup solutions for the focusing energy supercritical nonlinear Schrödinger equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34448)
*Xuanying Li*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper constructs radial finite-time type II blow-up solutions for the focusing energy-supercritical NLS in dimensions d≥11 under the Joseph–Lundgren condition, concentrating the ground state Q with a power-law scale λ(t)=t^{-1/2-ν}, while all subcritical homogeneous Sobolev norms stay bounded and radiation converges to a limiting profile.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Focusing energy-supercritical NLS admits type II blow-up whose structure is poorly understood, and prior constructions did not cover this regime (d≥11, Joseph–Lundgren condition). The aim is to rigorously establish a family of concentrating blow-up solutions and characterize their radiation and regularity behavior.

Method: Matched asymptotic expansions across three spatial scales combined with modified energy estimates; systematic treatment of the singular nonlinear background and the coupled oscillations induced by the slow algebraic tail of the ground state Q.

Result: For every ν>ν_0(d,p) a radial finite-time type II blow-up solution of the form ψ=λ^{1/p}Q(λx)+ζ exists with λ(t)=t^{-1/2-ν}; all subcritical homogeneous Sobolev norms remain bounded, and the radiation converges to ζ* in Ḣ^s for s_c<s<s_c+2ν|λ_+|, with λ_+ set by the leading correction to Q's asymptotic expansion.

Conclusion: A robust family of type II blow-up solutions concentrating Q is established in the supercritical regime via scale-matched asymptotics and refined energy methods, clarifying the role of the ground state's algebraic tail and identifying the radiation's limiting regularity range.

Abstract: We consider the focusing energy supercritical nonlinear Schrödinger equation \begin{equation*} i\partial_tψ+Δψ+|ψ|^{2p}ψ=0 \end{equation*} in dimensions $d\geq11$, with $p\in\mathbb N^\ast$, under the Joseph--Lundgren condition $2p+1>p_{JL}(d)$. For every $ν>ν_0(d,p)$, there exists a radial finite-time type II blow-up solution concentrating the ground state $Q$ in the form \begin{equation*} ψ(t,x)=λ^{\frac{1}{p}}(t)Q(λ(t)x)+ζ(t,x),\quad λ(t)=t^{-\frac12-ν}. \end{equation*} All homogeneous Sobolev norms $\|ψ(t)\|_{\dot H^s}$, $1\leq s<s_c$, remain bounded. The radiation converges to a limiting profile \begin{equation*}
  ζ^*\in \dot H^s(\mathbb R^d), \qquad s_c<s<s_c+2ν|λ_+|, \end{equation*} where $λ_+$ is determined by the leading correction in the asymptotic expansion of $Q$ at infinity. Our proof combines matched asymptotic expansions across three spatial scales with modified energy estimates. The key ingredient is the systematic treatment of the singular nonlinear background and coupled oscillations generated by the slow algebraic tail of $Q$.

</details>


### [79] [Regularity of the solution map for the Oldroyd--B model with fractional stress dissipation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34498)
*Gerard Misiołek,Jiahong Wu,Mingyu Yu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: For the incompressible Oldroyd-B model in high-Re/high-We regimes, the solution map is Lipschitz (uniformly in time) from small H^s data to H^r with r=s-(2β-1), behaving like Navier-Stokes rather than Euler, despite velocity being only transported.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Euler's data-to-solution map is continuous but not uniformly continuous on Sobolev spaces, while viscosity makes Navier-Stokes Lipschitz on small data. The paper wants to locate a hybrid Oldroyd-B system between these extremes, where velocity is a forced Euler equation and the only dissipation is fractional stress diffusion.

Method: Uses a Lyapunov functional with cross term (δu, ∇·δτ)_{H^{r-β}} that transfers stress dissipation to the velocity difference, exploiting the damped-wave structure from u–∇·τ coupling; plus a low-frequency interpolation argument keeping Gronwall coefficients integrable on (0,∞).

Result: On the ball of small H^s data (s>1+d/2) the solution map is Lipschitz H^s→H^r, r=s-(2β-1), uniformly in time, with explicit constant →√3 as data→0; hence Hölder into C_b([0,∞);H^{s'}) for s'<s, and Lipschitz in H^s when β=1/2. The 2β-1 derivative loss is exactly the gap between hidden velocity dissipation of order 2(1-β) and that required, and cannot be reduced by the energy method.

Conclusion: Although velocity is only transported, the Oldroyd-B solution map behaves like Navier-Stokes rather than Euler; the regularity gap and its optimality under the energy method are characterized.

Abstract: The data-to-solution map of the incompressible Euler equations is continuous but not uniformly continuous on Sobolev spaces, whereas viscosity makes the Navier--Stokes solution map Lipschitz continuous on small data, uniformly in time. This paper identifies the behavior of the solution map for a hybrid system sitting between these two extremes: the incompressible Oldroyd--B model in $\mathbb{R}^d$, $d=2,3$, in the high-Reynolds-number, high-Weissenberg-number regime. The velocity equation is a forced Euler equation and the only dissipation is a fractional diffusion $η(-Δ)^β$, $\frac12\leβ\le1$, acting on stress. We prove that, on the ball of small $H^s$ data ($s>1+\frac d2$) on which global solutions exist, the solution map is Lipschitz continuous with respect to the $H^{r}$-norm, $r=s-(2β-1)$, uniformly for all time, and Lipschitz into the natural dissipation spaces; the constant is explicit and tends to $\sqrt3$ as the size of the data tends to zero. Consequently the map is Hölder continuous into $C_b([0,\infty);H^{s'})$ for every $s'<s$, and is Lipschitz in $H^s$ itself when $β=\frac12$. Thus, although the velocity field is only transported, the solution map behaves like that of the Navier--Stokes equations rather than that of the Euler equations. The proof exploits the damped wave structure generated by the coupling between $u$ and $\nabla\cdotτ$ through a Lyapunov functional with a cross term $(δu,\nabla\cdotδτ)_{H^{r-β}}$, which transfers the stress dissipation to the velocity difference, together with a low-frequency interpolation argument that keeps all Gronwall coefficients integrable on $(0,\infty)$. The loss of $2β-1$ derivatives is exactly the gap between the hidden velocity dissipation of order $2(1-β)$ and the dissipation required by the Lipschitz estimate, and we show that it cannot be reduced by the energy method.

</details>


### [80] [Mixing zone dynamics for the Saffman-Taylor instability with viscosity jump](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34504)
*Paula Luna-Velasco,Francisco Mengual*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The authors construct mixing solutions to the incompressible porous media (Muskat) equation for fully unstable initial data, with a mixing zone that expands linearly in time around an evolving central interface, by combining the h-principle with inversion of the Birkhoff-Rott operator.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Fully unstable Muskat initial data lead to ill-posed dynamics where classical solutions break down, motivating the need to understand the behavior of the system through weak/mixing solutions.

Method: Combines the h-principle (convex integration) with inversion of the Birkhoff-Rott operator, which is necessary when viscosities differ. A key novelty is formulating this inversion in terms of the Saffman-Taylor function rather than the vorticity.

Result: Obtains mixing solutions whose mixing zone expands linearly in time around an evolving central interface.

Conclusion: Using the Saffman-Taylor function instead of vorticity greatly simplifies the analysis, and this reformulation may be of independent interest for the broader Muskat problem.

Abstract: We construct mixing solutions to the incompressible porous media equation for fully unstable Muskat initial data. The mixing zone expands linearly in time around an evolving central interface. Our approach combines the $h$-principle with the inversion of the associated Birkhoff-Rott operator, required when the viscosities differ. A key ingredient is to formulate this inversion in terms of the Saffman-Taylor function rather than the vorticity. This considerably simplifies the analysis and may be of independent interest for the Muskat problem.

</details>


### [81] [Lax Pair for the Kirchhoff-Pohozaev equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34522)
*Dario Bambusi,Emanuele Haus,Simone Marrocco,Michela Procesi*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper constructs a Lax pair (L,B) for the Kirchhoff-Pohozaev equation on any compact connected boundaryless Riemannian manifold, with L a self-adjoint finite-rank perturbation of the Laplace-Beltrami operator and B skew-adjoint of finite rank, both analytic in phase space. Using the perturbative determinant, the authors show the previously constructed integrals of motion admit a Lax pair formulation with the same L, yielding a new proof of their involutivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper aims to give a Lax pair formulation for the Kirchhoff-Pohozaev equation and to provide a new proof of involutivity of the integrals of motion constructed earlier (Boiti-Manfrin 2026, BHMP, Campos).

Method: The authors construct a Lax pair (L,B) where L is a self-adjoint finite-rank perturbation of the Laplace-Beltrami operator and B is a finite-rank skew-adjoint operator, both analytic in phase space variables. They study the spectrum of L via a perturbative determinant with a product representation analogous to the KdV generating function.

Result: The Kirchhoff-Pohozaev equation on any compact connected boundaryless Riemannian manifold admits a Lax pair, and the integrals of motion admit a Lax pair formulation with the same operator L. A new proof of the involutivity of these integrals is obtained.

Conclusion: The Kirchhoff-Pohozaev equation is integrable in the Lax sense on general compact boundaryless Riemannian manifolds, and the perturbative determinant approach yields a clean new proof of the involutivity of the known integrals of motion.

Abstract: We prove that the Kirchhoff-Pohozaev equation on any compact connected Riemannian manifold without boundary admits a Lax pair $(L,B)$, where $L$ is a self-adjoint finite-rank perturbation of the Laplace-Beltrami operator and $B$ is a skew-adjoint operator of finite rank, both depending analytically on the phase space variables. We study the spectrum of $L$ using the perturbative determinant which is known to determine its spectrum and to admit a product representation similar to that of the generating function in KdV. We use this to show that the dynamics of the integrals of motion constructed in \cite{BoitiManfrin2026,BHMP,campos} admits a Lax pair formulation with the same operator $L$ and to get a new proof of the involutivity of these integrals.

</details>


### [82] [Long-time action stability for perturbed KdV near finite-gap tori](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34601)
*Guan Huang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves action and spectral stability near finite-gap tori of the periodic KdV equation under small analytic Hamiltonian perturbations, uniformly over compact families without small-amplitude or nonresonance assumptions, allowing infinitely many open gaps. Coercivity of the negative Hessian plus resonance analysis and corrected actions yield quantitative long-time stability bounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Finite-gap tori of the periodic KdV equation are integrable structures, but stability under generic Hamiltonian perturbations is delicate—existing results often need small-amplitude or nonresonance conditions and exclude data with infinitely many open gaps. The authors aim for robust, uniform stability estimates that avoid these restrictions.

Method: They establish coercivity of the negative Hessian of the nonlinear KdV Hamiltonian on the full l^2 action space at every finite-gap action, then combine resonance analysis with corrected action observables evaluated along the solution. Under a strong-solution and uniform higher Sobolev bound on [0, T0/ε], they allow one-derivative loss in the perturbing Hamiltonian's normal gradient and derive orbit-following estimates for actions and spectral gaps.

Result: For small ε and initial normal Birkhoff size r0, tangential action variations and the weighted sum of normal actions are bounded by C_δ(r0^2 + ε^{2δ}), and the l^2 spectral-gap variation by C_δ(r0 + ε^δ), for every 0<δ<1/4. Hypotheses are verified for several local and nonlocal Hamiltonian perturbations.

Conclusion: The work shows uniform action and spectral stability near finite-gap KdV tori under a broad class of analytic perturbations, with the Hessian coercivity providing the central mechanism and the results holding even with infinitely many open gaps and one-derivative loss.

Abstract: We prove action and spectral stability near finite-gap tori of the periodic Korteweg--de Vries equation under autonomous analytic Hamiltonian perturbations of size $\varepsilon$. The estimates are uniform over prescribed compact families, without small-amplitude or nonresonance assumptions, and allow nearby initial data with infinitely many open gaps. Assuming a strong solution and a uniform higher Sobolev bound on $[0,T_0/\varepsilon]$, we permit one-derivative loss in the perturbing Hamiltonian's normal gradient. For sufficiently small $\varepsilon$ and initial normal Birkhoff size $r_0$, the tangential action variations and weighted sum of normal actions are bounded by $C_δ(r_0^2+\varepsilon^{2δ})$, while the spectral-gap variation in $\ell^2$ is bounded by $C_δ(r_0+\varepsilon^δ)$, for every $0<δ<1/4$. A key ingredient is coercivity of the negative Hessian of the nonlinear KdV Hamiltonian on the full $\ell^2$ action space at every finite-gap action. We combine this with resonance analysis and corrected action observables evaluated along the solution. The hypotheses are verified for several local and nonlocal Hamiltonian perturbations.

</details>


### [83] [The Inhomogeneous Kinetic FPUT Equation near Rayleigh-Jeans Equilibria](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34636)
*Angeliki Menegaki,Haoling Xiang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves global well-posedness and asymptotic stability of nonsingular Rayleigh-Jeans equilibria for the spatially inhomogeneous kinetic wave equation of the FPUT-β problem, via spectral analysis and energy estimates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The nonlinear dynamics of the spatially inhomogeneous kinetic wave equation for FPUT-β near Rayleigh-Jeans equilibria remain poorly understood, particularly since the collision operator lacks a spectral gap, making standard stability arguments unavailable.

Method: Combines a spectral analysis of the linearized operator near the origin at small spatial frequencies (identifying eigenvalues of orders |ξ|^{8/5} and ξ^2) with energy estimates that account for the transport effect.

Result: Establishes algebraic decay for the linear semigroup despite the absence of a spectral gap, then uses these estimates to obtain global-in-time well-posedness and asymptotic stability in weighted L∞ norms in space and momentum, for small perturbations orthogonal to the collision kernel.

Conclusion: The spatially inhomogeneous kinetic wave equation for FPUT-β is globally well-posed and asymptotically stable near nonsingular Rayleigh-Jeans equilibria, with algebraic decay driven by combined spectral and transport effects.

Abstract: We study the nonlinear dynamics of the spatially inhomogeneous kinetic wave equation associated with the FPUT-$β$ problem. We prove global-in-time well-posedness near nonsingular Rayleigh-Jeans equilibria and their asymptotic stability in particular in weighted $L^\infty$ norms in space and momentum, for small initial perturbations orthogonal to the collision kernel at almost every spatial point. We describe the spectrum near the origin at small spatial frequencies, identifying two simple eigenvalues with fractional and diffusive orders $|ξ|^{8/5}$ and $ξ^2$, respectively. Combining this spectral analysis with energy estimates taking into account the transport effect, we establish algebraic decay for the linear semigroup despite the absence of a spectral gap for the collision operator. We then use these estimates to obtain nonlinear stability.

</details>


### [84] [A Deterministic Game-Theoretic Approach to Convexity-Preserving Property and Finite-Time Extinction of Level Sets for Geometric Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34671)
*Yousuke Saga,Kazuhiro Takimoto*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Using a game-theoretic (Kohn–Serfaty/Sato) approach, this paper studies convexity preservation and finite-time extinction of level sets for quasilinear geometric equations, building a tug-of-war game whose value function approximates the viscosity solution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to understand qualitative behavior—convexity preservation and finite-time extinction of level sets—of quasilinear geometric equations, using a probabilistic/game interpretation rather than purely analytic PDE methods.

Method: They adopt the game-theoretic framework of Kohn and Serfaty and, following Sato's approach, construct a tug-of-war-type stochastic game whose value function approximates the viscosity solution of the geometric equation; properties are then derived from the game.

Result: The game-theoretic construction yields a value function approximating the viscosity solution, enabling the derivation of the convexity-preserving property and finite-time extinction of level sets.
