Conclusion: The Kohn–Serfaty/Sato game approach provides an effective tool for establishing convexity preservation and finite-time extinction for quasilinear geometric equations, complementing analytic techniques.

Abstract: We consider the convexity-preserving property and finite-time extinction of level sets for quasilinear geometric equations using a game-theoretic approach introduced by Kohn and Serfaty. To construct the game whose value function approximates the viscosity solution, we use the approach introduced by Sato.

</details>


### [85] [Unconditional uniqueness for the cubic nonlinear Schrödinger equation in $H^{1/2}(\Bbb T^3)$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34733)
*Yongming Luo,Jiayu Zheng*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves unconditional uniqueness for the cubic NLS in the scaling-critical space H^{1/2}(T^3), showing every distributional solution in C_t H_x^{1/2} a priori lies in L^2_{t,loc} L_x^6, using new Euclidean-like Strichartz estimates rather than GP-hierarchy or normal form reductions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing approaches to uniqueness (GP-hierarchy, normal form reductions) rely on higher-order expansions. The authors want a proof that works directly with the original cubic NLS at the scaling-critical regularity H^{1/2}(T^3).

Method: A direct argument using new a priori Euclidean-like Strichartz estimates, a high-low modulation decomposition, and compactness of solution trajectories on bounded time intervals. Stability estimates in the rough space H^{-1/2}(T^3) are obtained by exploiting preservation of the canonical symplectic form under the truncated NLS flow.

Result: Unconditional uniqueness is established: every distributional solution in C_t H_x^{1/2} belongs a priori to L^2_{t,loc} L_x^6, giving uniqueness without auxiliary conditions.

Conclusion: Unconditional uniqueness for cubic NLS at critical regularity on T^3 can be proven directly, avoiding higher-order expansion techniques, via Strichartz estimates, modulation decomposition, compactness, and symplectic-form preservation.

Abstract: In this paper, we establish unconditional uniqueness for the cubic nonlinear Schrödinger equation (NLS) in the scaling-critical class $H^{1/2}(\mathbb{T}^3)$. Unlike approaches based on the GP-hierarchy or normal form reductions, which typically involve higher-order expansions, our proof works directly with the original cubic NLS, where we also make use of several new a priori Euclidean-like Strichartz estimates. More precisely, we show that every distributional solution in $C_tH_x^{1/2}$ belongs a priori to the class $L^2_{t,\mathrm{loc}}L_x^6$. The key ingredients are a suitable high-low modulation decomposition and the compactness of solution trajectories on bounded time intervals. We also exploit the preservation of the canonical symplectic form under the truncated NLS flow, in order to obtain necessary stability estimates in the rough Sobolev space $H^{-1/2}(\mathbb{T}^3)$.

</details>


### [86] [Propagation dynamics of a parabolic-elliptic chemotaxis model in a time-periodic shifting environment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34753)
*Shuwen Xue,Xiao-Qiang Zhao*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies propagation dynamics of a parabolic-elliptic chemotaxis model in a spatially separated, time-periodically shifting habitat, establishing vanishing/persistence/spreading criteria and existence/nonexistence of time-periodic forced waves.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Species face environments where favorable and unfavorable habitats are spatially separated and the favorable region shifts over time (e.g., climate change). Understanding when a chemotactic population can track such a shifting habitat—versus going extinct—is largely unexplored for chemotaxis models with periodic shifting speeds.

Method: Analyzes a parabolic-elliptic chemotaxis model in a shifting environment where the favorable habitat moves with a time-periodic speed. Derives vanishing, persistence, and spreading properties for compactly supported and front-like initial data, comparing self-limitation rate against chemotactic sensitivity and relating outcomes to the average shifting speed. Also proves existence and nonexistence of time-periodic forced waves with speed equal to the average shifting speed.

Result: If self-limitation is large relative to chemotactic sensitivity and the species starts in a bounded region, it goes extinct when the habitat shifts rightward with a large average speed. For moderate average shifting speeds, the species persists and spreads along the shifting habitat into an ever-growing spatial region. Time-periodic forced waves traveling at the average shifting speed exist or fail to exist depending on parameters.

Conclusion: The model exhibits a sharp transition—governed by shifting speed, self-limitation, and chemotaxis strength—between extinction and persistence/spreading in a shifting habitat, with forced periodic waves characterizing the critical propagation regime.

Abstract: The current paper is devoted to the study of the propagation dynamics of a parabolic-elliptic chemotaxis model in a shifting environment. We investigate the scenario in which the favorable and unfavorable habitats are spatially separated, with the favorable habitat shifting at a time-periodic speed. We first establish the vanishing, persistence, and spreading properties of solutions with compactly supported and front-like initial data. In particular, we show that if the self-limitation rate of the species is large relative to the chemotactic sensitivity and the species is initially confined to a bounded region, then the species eventually becomes extinct when the habitat shifts rightward with a large average speed. In contrast, when the average shifting speed of the habitat is moderate, the species can persist and spread along the shifting habitat into an increasingly large spatial region. Finally, we establish the existence and nonexistence of time-periodic forced wave solutions whose wave speed coincides with the average shifting speed of the habitat.

</details>


### [87] [Liouville theorems for the stationary Navier-Stokes equations under partial component conditions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34780)
*Cuiyang Liu,Wei Luo*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves Liouville-type theorems for smooth stationary incompressible Navier-Stokes flows in R^3 with finite Dirichlet integral and velocity vanishing at infinity: if (u1,u2) in L^{4,inf}, or just u1 in L^{3/2,inf}, then u=0.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Strengthen Liouville/triviality results for stationary Navier-Stokes solutions by requiring weaker integrability assumptions on velocity components rather than the full velocity, leveraging only partial-component conditions.

Method: Proof based on the sign of the head pressure and a localized energy identity, combined with different pressure estimates for the two cases (L^{4,inf} for two components; L^{3/2,inf} for one component).

Result: Two Liouville theorems: vanishing of u when (u1,u2) in L^{4,inf}; and vanishing when a single component u1 in L^{3/2,inf}.

Conclusion: Partial-component weak-Lebesgue conditions suffice to force stationary finite-energy Navier-Stokes flows in R^3 to be trivial, extending classical Liouville results.

Abstract: We prove Liouville theorems for smooth stationary incompressible Navier-Stokes flows in $R^3$ with finite Dirichlet integral and velocity vanishing uniformly at infinity. If the partial components of velocity $(u_1,u_2)$ belong to $L^{4,\infty}$, then we show that $u$ must vanish. The second result only requires that one component $u_1$ belongs to $L^{3/2,\infty}$. The proofs are based on the head-pressure sign and a localized energy identity, combined with different pressure estimates for the two cases.

</details>


### [88] [Designed short-time observations for rotational damping recovery in Timoshenko beams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34786)
*Mohammad El-Hindi*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies recovery of a spatial rotation-dependent damping coefficient in a clamped Timoshenko beam from a single spatial average of rotation, measured across labelled experiments with designed initial rotational velocities. The leading coefficient-to-data map is made an invertible Gram matrix, yielding global injectivity, Lipschitz stability, and globally convergent reconstruction, with matching T^{-5/2} noise bounds and numerical validation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Identifying spatially varying damping in beam models is an inverse problem, but standard approaches require differentiating the damping coefficient or rely on multiple/dense measurements. The authors seek a provably stable, globally convergent recovery method using only limited data (one spatial average of rotation) with carefully designed experiments, avoiding differentiated coefficients.

Method: The coefficient lies in a prescribed finite-dimensional bounded family. Initial rotational velocities are constructed so the leading coefficient-to-data map becomes an invertible Gram matrix. Testing the equations against a smooth observation kernel yields a uniform parameter-Lipschitz remainder without differentiating the damping. A weighted temporal moment then gives global injectivity and Lipschitz stability, plus a projected fixed-point reconstruction. Continuous and exact discrete adjoints are derived; a conforming finite-element midpoint scheme with finite-time convergence is proved, and forward error is propagated to coefficient error.

Result: Establishes global injectivity and Lipschitz stability for sufficiently short observation times, with globally convergent projected fixed-point reconstruction. Upper and deterministic two-point lower bounds give the same noise-amplification order T^{-5/2} for L² time-history errors. A second sensitivity estimate provides an alternative sufficient condition for strong convexity and global projected-gradient convergence. Synthetic experiments assess symmetry, gradient accuracy, noise, and discretization bias.

Conclusion: The work provides a rigorous, computationally tractable framework for identifying spatial rotational damping in a Timoshenko beam from minimal averaged data, with provable stability, optimal-order noise bounds, convergent discretization, and reproducibility via synthetic tests.

Abstract: We study the recovery of spatial rotational damping in a clamped Timoshenko beam. The coefficient belongs to a prescribed bounded finite-dimensional family. One fixed spatial average of rotation is recorded in separately labelled experiments with known initial rotational velocities. We construct these velocities so that the leading coefficient-to-data map is an invertible Gram matrix. Testing the equations against the smooth observation kernel gives a uniform parameter-Lipschitz remainder without differentiating the damping coefficient. A weighted temporal moment then yields global injectivity, Lipschitz stability and a globally convergent projected fixed-point reconstruction for sufficiently short observation times. Upper and deterministic two-point lower bounds give the same noise-amplification order $T^{-5/2}$ for $L^2$ time-history errors. A second sensitivity estimate supplies a separate sufficient condition for strong convexity and global projected-gradient convergence. We derive continuous and exact discrete adjoints, prove finite-time convergence of a conforming finite-element midpoint scheme, and propagate forward error into coefficient error. Reproducible synthetic experiments examine symmetry, gradient accuracy, noise and discretization bias.

</details>


### [89] [Endpoint well-posedness for the Boussinesq equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34793)
*Mingjuan Chen,Minjie Shan*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Small-data global well-posedness is proved for the quadratic "good" Boussinesq equation on both the real line and the circle at the endpoint regularity H^{-1/2} × H^{-3/2}.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The quadratic good Boussinesq equation is classically studied at regularities above the endpoint; establishing well-posedness at the critical endpoint H^{-1/2} × H^{-3/2} was an open threshold question on both domains.

Method: A priori bounds come from a balanced perturbation determinant of the third-order Lax operator; averaging two real spectral branches gives coercivity and frequency-tail control. On the line, the endpoint flow is built via commuting Hamiltonians and Green-field estimates; on the circle, discrete spectral bounds combined with Kishimoto's endpoint local theory yield global well-posedness.

Result: A small invariant spectral set supports a continuous global group with uniqueness in a fixed local integral class; in the periodic case both components have zero mean and the flow is Lipschitz on bounded time intervals.

Conclusion: The endpoint regularity is attainable for the quadratic good Boussinesq equation on both the real line and the circle, with the two settings requiring distinct completion strategies (spectral/Green-field on the line, discrete bounds plus Kishimoto theory on the circle).

Abstract: We prove small-data global well-posedness for the quadratic good Boussinesq equation on the real line and the circle at the endpoint $H^{-1/2}\times H^{-3/2}$. On each domain, a small invariant spectral set supports a continuous global group, with uniqueness in a fixed local integral class. In the periodic case, both components have zero mean and the flow is Lipschitz on bounded time intervals. The a priori bounds follow from a balanced perturbation determinant for the third-order Lax operator. Averaging two real spectral branches yields coercivity and frequency-tail control. On the line, we construct the endpoint flow by commuting Hamiltonians and Green-field estimates. On the circle, discrete spectral bounds and Kishimoto's endpoint local theory give global well-posedness.

</details>


### [90] [Linear Stability, Inviscid Damping, and Scattering for Monotone Shear Flows in 2D Ideal MHD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34867)
*Zhile Li*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Sharp long-time asymptotics and scattering are proved for the linearized 2D ideal MHD equations around a slowly varying monotone shear flow with a constant horizontal magnetic field, with explicit paired decay/growth rates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior results on linearized Euler/MHD shear flows typically require U' close to a constant or rely on particular profiles; here they aim to handle general monotone shear flows whose shear rate is merely bounded above and below by positive constants and varies slowly.

Method: Linearize 2D ideal incompressible MHD around (U(y),0) with (β,0) on the torus-cross-line domain for fixed β≠0; study the linear evolution of zero-horizontal-average perturbations, deriving sharp L^2 time rates and proving scattering to Alfvén profiles, plus asymptotic completeness in the natural energy space.

Result: For every nontrivial perturbation with zero horizontal average: horizontal velocity and magnetic components stay O(1), vertical components decay like &lt;t&gt;^{-1}, and the vorticity–current pair grows like &lt;t&gt;. Strong L^2 scattering to two Alfvén profiles holds, the leading asymptotics of physical variables are identified, and asymptotic completeness holds in the energy space.

Conclusion: The linearized MHD operator exhibits a sharp paired-norm dichotomy (bounded/decaying/growing components) with complete scattering theory, extending inviscid damping-type results to slowly varying monotone shear flows with a constant background magnetic field.

Abstract: We study the linearized 2D ideal incompressible magnetohydrodynamic equations around a monotone shear flow $(U(y),0)$ with a constant horizontal magnetic field $(β,0)$ on $\mathbb{T} \times \mathbb{R}$. For each fixed $β\neq 0$, we assume that the shear rate $U'$ is bounded above and below by positive constants and varies sufficiently slowly, although it need not be close to any constant. For every nontrivial perturbation with zero horizontal average, we establish sharp $L^2$ time rates in the corresponding paired norms: the horizontal velocity and magnetic components remain of order one, the vertical components decay at rate $\langle t \rangle^{-1}$, and the vorticity--current pair grows at rate $\langle t \rangle$. We prove strong $L^2$ scattering to two Alfvén profiles, identify the leading asymptotic behavior of the physical variables, and establish asymptotic completeness in the natural energy space.

</details>


### [91] [Effective Hamiltonians in Perforated Domains: Inf-Max formula and Stability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34910)
*Yuxi Han,Son N. T. Tu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies how the effective (homogenized) Hamiltonian for periodic perforated domains with state-constraint boundary conditions depends on the domain geometry, proving an inf-max representation and a quantitative stability estimate.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: For convex Hamilton-Jacobi equations with state-constraint boundary conditions on periodic perforated domains, the effective Hamiltonian's dependence on the geometry of the holes is poorly understood; the goal is to quantify this dependence and determine when domain approximations preserve the effective Hamiltonian.

Method: An inf-max representation of the effective Hamiltonian over smooth test functions is established for domains with C^1 boundary. This representation is used to derive a quantitative stability estimate in terms of the Hausdorff distance between domains and the reach of one boundary.

Result: The stability estimate gives convergence for non-nested domain approximations whose Hausdorff error is negligible relative to their boundary reach. Examples show the continuity modulus is sharp and that Hausdorff convergence alone can fail to preserve the effective Hamiltonian, even for smooth domains and the quadratic Hamiltonian.

Conclusion: Effective Hamiltonians are stable under domain perturbations controlled jointly by Hausdorff distance and boundary reach, but not under Hausdorff convergence alone; the inf-max representation and estimate provide a sharp framework for such geometric stability.

Abstract: We study the dependence of the effective Hamiltonian $\overline H_Ω(p)$ on the geometry of periodic perforated domains for convex Hamilton--Jacobi equations with state-constraint boundary conditions. We establish an inf--max representation over smooth test functions for domains with $C^1$ boundary. Using this representation, we derive a quantitative stability estimate in terms of the Hausdorff distance between domains and the reach of one boundary. This estimate yields convergence for non-nested domain approximations whose Hausdorff error is negligible relative to their boundary reach. Examples demonstrate the sharpness of the continuity modulus and show that Hausdorff convergence alone can fail to preserve the effective Hamiltonian, even for smooth domains and the quadratic Hamiltonian.

</details>


### [92] [The Sharp Constant in the Second-Order Hydrogen Uncertainty Principle: Spectral Characterization and Quantitative Approximation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34916)
*Mohamed Jleli,Bessem Samet*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper characterizes the sharp constant H_d in the second-order Hydrogen Uncertainty Principle for all dimensions d≥2 via a spectral reformulation, recovering known values for d≥4 and providing new effective bounds for d=2,3.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The sharp constant in the second-order Hydrogen Uncertainty Principle is only known for certain dimensions (d≥4) and poorly estimated for d=2,3; a unified understanding across all dimensions is missing.

Method: Uses a spectral basis from generalized Laguerre polynomials, a linearization procedure, and spherical harmonic decomposition to obtain a spectral characterization. Defines an explicit positive Hilbert–Schmidt operator S_c^(d) on ℓ²(N_0) and shows H_d = ½ min{d+1, c_d^*} where c_d^* is the unique parameter with ‖S_{c_d^*}^(d)‖=1.

Result: Proof that H_d = ½ min{d+1, c_d^*}, revealing competition between the radial sector (value d+1) and first non-radial sector. For d≥4, recovers H_d=(d+1)/2, giving an alternative proof for d≥5 (Cazacu–Flynn–Lam) and d=4 (Huang–Ye). For d∈{2,3}, H_d=c_d^*/2.

Conclusion: The spectral formulation yields an explicit finite recursive scheme giving effective two-sided bounds for H_2 and H_3, substantially improving Huang–Ye's estimates.

Abstract: In this work, we study the sharp constant \(\mathcal H_d\) in the second-order Hydrogen Uncertainty Principle in every dimension \(d\ge2\). Using a spectral basis built from generalized Laguerre polynomials, together with a linearization procedure and spherical harmonic decomposition, we establish a spectral characterization of \(\mathcal H_d\). More precisely, we show that \[ \mathcal H_d =
\frac12\min\{d+1,c_d^*\}, \] where \(c_d^*\in(0,d+3)\) is the unique parameter such that \[ \|S_{c_d^*}^{(d)}\|_{\ell^2\to\ell^2}=1. \] Here, for \(0<c<d+3\), \(S_c^{(d)}\) is an explicit positive Hilbert--Schmidt operator on \(\ell^2(\mathbb N_0)\). This characterization reveals a competition between the radial sector, which yields the value \(d+1\), and the first non-radial sector, encoded by the spectral parameter \(c_d^*\). For \(d\ge4\), we recover the known identity \[ \mathcal H_d=\frac{d+1}{2} \] through a spectral argument, giving an alternative proof of the results of Cazacu, Flynn and Lam \cite{CazacuFlynnLam} for \(d\ge5\) and Huang and Ye \cite{HuangYe} for \(d=4\). For \(d\in\{2,3\}\), this characterization yields \[ \mathcal H_d=\frac{c_d^*}{2}. \] We then exploit this spectral formulation to derive an explicit finite recursive scheme yielding effective two-sided bounds for \(\mathcal H_2\) and \(\mathcal H_3\), substantially improving the estimates of Huang and Ye \cite{HuangYe}.

</details>


### [93] [Development of singular surfaces and formation of generic singularities in 3D pressureless flows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34958)
*Geng Chen,Faris El-Katri,Shoujun Huang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper analyzes shock (singularity) formation for 3D pressureless Euler (sticky particle) equations from smooth initial data, characterizing the singular hypersurface formed after blow-up time $t_0$ and constructing local analytic solutions via a Briot–Bouquet-type reformulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Smooth solutions of pressureless flows develop unbounded density/velocity gradients at a finite time, linked to sticky-particle dynamics; while the 2D case was treated by Bressan–Chen–Huang, the 3D structure and the nature of the post-blow-up singular hypersurface were not understood, and its governing system is not hyperbolic.

Method: For an open dense set of smooth initial data, transversality theorem plus constructive initial velocity fields to establish formation and structure of singularities. Assuming analytic initial data, analyze singular behavior of velocity and density, then recast the singular-hypersurface equations as a first-order Briot–Bouquet system and solve locally via a Cauchy–Kovalevskaya-type theorem.

Result: For generic smooth data, singularities form and, for $t>t_0$, a singular hypersurface emerges whose governing equations are non-hyperbolic (contrasting the 2D case). For analytic data, the precise singular behavior of velocity and density is characterized and the hypersurface system admits local analytic solutions.

Conclusion: The 3D pressureless flow develops a singular hypersurface after blow-up whose dynamics is non-hyperbolic; analyticity enables a Briot–Bouquet reduction and local existence of analytic solutions, extending the 2D theory of Bressan–Chen–Huang to three dimensions.

Abstract: In this paper, the Cauchy problem for the equations of pressureless flows in three space dimensions is considered. It is well known that for smooth initial data, the density and velocity gradient often become unbounded at a finite time $t_0$. This type of singularity is associated with sticky-particle dynamics. For an open dense set of smooth initial data, the formation and structure of singularities are investigated using the transversality theorem, with the aid of constructive methods for initial velocity fields. In contrast to the two-dimensional case studied by Bressan, Chen and Huang (arXiv:2408.06877), for $t>t_0$ a singular hypersurface will be formed, whose governing system of equations is not hyperbolic. By assuming the initial data are analytic, we are able to precisely analyze the singular behavior of velocity and density. Based on this, the system of equations for singular hypersurfaces can be recast into a first-order system of Briot-Bouquet type, and the local analytic solution can be constructed using a version of the Cauchy-Kovalevskaya theorem.

</details>


### [94] [On Brezis' open problem 2.4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34979)
*Hong-Ge Chen,Yong Liu,Juncheng Wei,Wen Yang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper constructs a nonradial smooth entire solution of the Ginzburg-Landau equation with degree q in {8,9} whose modulus tends to one at infinity, thereby giving a negative answer to Brezis' Open Problem 2.4.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Brezis' Open Problem 2.4 asked whether entire solutions of the Ginzburg-Landau equation with modulus tending to one at infinity must be radial. The authors aim to resolve this question.

Method: They vary a cone parameter q in [8,9] while keeping the degree on the cone equal to one. Key ingredients include computing the Morse index of radial vortex solutions at q in {8,9}, establishing compactness, and applying Leray-Schauder degree theory together with a homotopy argument.

Result: They prove existence of a nonradial smooth entire solution of the Ginzburg-Landau equation with degree q in {8,9} and modulus tending to one at infinity, answering Brezis' Open Problem 2.4 negatively.

Conclusion: Nonradial entire solutions do exist for degrees 8 and 9, so the radial-symmetry expectation underlying Brezis' open problem fails.

Abstract: We show the existence of a ``nonradial" smooth entire solution of the Ginzburg--Landau equation with degree $q\in\{8,9\}$ and modulus tending to one at infinity. This answers Brezis' Open Problem 2.4 negatively. To construct the counterexample, we vary a cone parameter $q \in [8, 9]$ and keeping the degree on the cone equal to one. Several key ingredients are needed. First, we need to compute the Morse index of radial vortex solutions at $q\in \{8, 9\}$. Second, we need to show compactness. Finally, Leray-Schauder degree theory and a homotopy argument yield the desired existence result.

</details>


### [95] [Singular set estimates in quasilinear Alt-Caffarelli problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34986)
*Antonio De Rosa,Robin Neumayer,Hui Yu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves that the singular set of the free boundary for nonnegative minimizers of a quasilinear Alt-Caffarelli energy has codimension at least 2+η, for a uniformly convex Φ, which is sharp in integer dimensions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The classical Alt-Caffarelli energy has a well-understood free boundary regularity theory, but the quasilinear generalization with a uniformly convex Φ is less studied. The authors aim to quantify the size of the singular set of the free boundary in this more general setting.

Method: They study nonnegative minimizers of ∫_Ω Φ(∇u) + χ_{u>0} under mild regularity assumptions on the uniformly convex Φ. Their proof method is flexible enough to be adapted to the p-Laplacian counterpart of the Alt-Caffarelli energy.

Result: They show the singular set of the free boundary has codimension at least 2+η for some positive η. Combined with a singular three-dimensional minimizer from forthcoming work, this bound is sharp when considering integer dimensions. A similar result holds for the p-Laplacian version of the energy.

Conclusion: The codimension estimate 2+η for the singular set is established for quasilinear Alt-Caffarelli minimizers and is sharp in integer dimensions; the method extends to the p-Laplacian analogue.

Abstract: For a nonnegative, uniformly convex function $Φ$, we study nonnegative minimizers of the quasilinear Alt-Caffarelli energy $\int_ΩΦ(\nabla u)+χ_{\{u>0\}}$. Under mild regularity assumptions on $Φ$, we show that the codimension of the singular set of the free boundary is at least $2+η$ for a positive $η$. With a singular minimizer in three dimensions in a forthcoming work, this result is sharp as long as integer dimensions are considered. Our proof method is flexible, and we use it to also prove a similar result for minimizers of the $p$-Laplacian counterpart of the Alt-Caffarelli energy.

</details>


### [96] [Inverse heat source problem from terminal data on a set of low Hausdorff dimension](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35014)
*Kai Yu,Zhiyuan Li,Zhidong Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies recovery of a spatial source in the heat equation from terminal observations on a lower-dimensional compact set $E$ (Hausdorff content dimension $\beta>d-1$), with forcing vanishing just before observation. It establishes conditional logarithmic stability, a sampling inequality for scattered data, and logarithmic convergence rates for Tikhonov reconstruction under noise.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Inverse source problems for the heat equation are typically studied with full boundary/interior observations, but practical measurements are limited to lower-dimensional or scattered sets. The authors aim to understand whether and how well a spatial source can be recovered from terminal data restricted to a fractal-type set of positive $\beta$-dimensional Hausdorff content, and to quantify stability and convergence under realistic sampling and noise.

Method: They impose a spectral Sobolev prior on the source and a quantitative noncancellation condition on the terminal spectral multipliers, and exploit the waiting interval where the forcing vanishes to obtain conditional logarithmic stability. For scattered observations on sets $E$ with $\beta>d-1$, they derive a sampling inequality under a quasi-uniformity assumption. They then analyze Tikhonov regularization with a data-driven parameter choice under explicit sampling and independent noise assumptions, and perform two numerical experiments using a finite candidate approximation of a fixed Hausdorff set.

Result: The authors prove conditional logarithmic stability for source recovery from terminal observations on $E$, obtain a sampling inequality for scattered measurements, and establish a logarithmic convergence rate in probability for the Tikhonov reconstructions with data-driven parameter selection. Numerical experiments assess discrete spectral sampling stability and show the influence of the waiting interval and measurement noise on reconstruction quality.

Conclusion: Source recovery in the heat equation from terminal data on lower-dimensional sets of Hausdorff content dimension $\beta>d-1$ is possible but only with logarithmic stability, reflecting the severe ill-posedness. The results provide a rigorous probabilistic convergence theory for Tikhonov reconstruction from scattered noisy data, and the numerics confirm the practical roles of the waiting interval and noise level.

Abstract: We study the recovery of a spatial source in the heat equation from terminal observations on a compact interior set $E$ with positive $β$ dimensional Hausdorff content for some $β>d-1$. The prescribed temporal forcing vanishes on a nontrivial interval immediately preceding the observation time. Under a spectral Sobolev prior and a quantitative noncancellation condition on the terminal spectral multipliers, we establish conditional logarithmic stability. For scattered observations on such sets, we derive a sampling inequality under a quasi uniformity assumption. Under explicit sampling and independent noise assumptions, we prove a logarithmic convergence rate in probability for the corresponding Tikhonov reconstructions with a data driven parameter choice. Two numerical experiments using a finite candidate approximation of a fixed Hausdorff set assess discrete spectral sampling stability and illustrate the effects of the waiting interval and measurement noise on reconstruction.

</details>


### [97] [Supercritical Geometric Optics for multidimensional scalar conservation laws](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35034)
*Christophe Cheverry,Rida Harb,St{é}phane Junca*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper analyzes propagation vs. damping of small-amplitude, high-frequency monophase oscillations near a smooth non-constant ground state for multidimensional scalar conservation laws, giving explicit criteria and WKB-expansion conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding how very high-frequency oscillations behave beyond the classical bounded-variation regime is key to clarifying smoothing effects for scalar conservation laws, including the fractional smoothing effect conjectured by Lions, Perthame and Tadmor.

Method: Study monophase oscillatory wave trains near a non-constant ground state; derive compatibility conditions distinguishing propagation from shock-formation damping; for higher frequencies, characterize propagation via Lie-algebra compatibility conditions under a local constant-rank assumption; construct WKB expansions and prove cancellation under first-order incompatibility.

Result: In the baseline supercritical regime, an explicit criterion separates propagation from damping with justified asymptotic descriptions. At higher frequencies, propagation is more restrictive, governed by Lie-algebra conditions, with a necessary and sufficient condition for complete WKB expansions. Non-constant backgrounds limit small-scale propagation relative to constant backgrounds.

Conclusion: The results clarify oscillatory propagation near non-constant states and contribute to understanding the Lions–Perthame–Tadmor fractional smoothing effect conjecture.

Abstract: This article studies the propagation, near a smooth non-constant ground state, of monophase oscillatory wave trains with small amplitude and very high frequency for multidimensional nonlinear scalar conservation laws. These oscillations lie beyond the classical regime of uniformly bounded variation. Two scenarios may occur. Under suitable compatibility conditions, the oscillations propagate. Otherwise, they are damped through shock formation. In the baseline supercritical regime, we give an explicit criterion distinguishing these two behaviors and justify the corresponding asymptotic descriptions. At higher frequencies, the propagation mechanism becomes more restrictive and is governed by compatibility conditions involving a Lie algebra. Under a natural local constant-rank assumption, we obtain a necessary and sufficient condition for the existence of complete WKB expansions. We also prove cancellation under first-order incompatibility. Perturbing a non-constant background state limits the propagation of small scales compared with the constant-background case. Our constructions contribute to the understanding of the fractional smoothing effect conjectured by Lions, Perthame and Tadmor.

</details>


### [98] [Type-II Singularities of Immersed Locally Convex Curves Evolving under Generalized Anisotropic Gage Area-Preserving Flows with Slow Curvature Speeds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35064)
*Ze-Yu Ye,Xiao-Liu Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper classifies finite-time singularities of locally convex closed plane curves under generalized anisotropic Gage area-preserving flows with slow curvature speeds $\kappa_\sigma^\alpha$ ($0<\alpha\le1$). It proves that any finite-time blow-up is Type-II whenever the Euclidean length stays bounded away from zero, and shows the rescaled curves converge to translating solitons of the anisotropic curvature flow $V=\kappa_\sigma^\alpha$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding singularity formation for area-preserving anisotropic curvature flows is important, but the regime of slow curvature speeds $\kappa_\sigma^\alpha$ with $\alpha\le1$ lacks a complete classification of blow-up types, especially without symmetry assumptions.

Method: The authors use Hamilton's rescaling method together with a differential Harnack estimate for the anisotropic flow $V=\kappa_\sigma^\alpha$, and analyze blow-up subsequences of the evolving curves to identify limiting profiles.

Result: Every finite-time blow-up is Type-II provided the Euclidean length remains uniformly bounded away from zero; this holds with no symmetry assumptions on the curve or anisotropy. Nonzero initial signed area guarantees this length bound, so all finite-time singularities are then Type-II. Rescaled curves converge locally to translating solution profiles of $V=\kappa_\sigma^\alpha$. Sufficient conditions for finite-time singularity formation are given via initial signed area and anisotropic length, and locally convex initial curves arbitrarily close to the Wulff curve are constructed exhibiting finite-time Type-II singularities.

Conclusion: The paper provides a symmetry-free Type-II classification of finite-time singularities for these anisotropic Gage flows, characterizes the blow-up limit as anisotropic translating solitons, and demonstrates that Type-II singularities can arise from curves arbitrarily close to the Wulff shape.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the singularity behavior of immersed locally convex closed plane curves evolving under generalized anisotropic Gage area-preserving flows with slow curvature speeds of the form $κ_σ^α$, where $0<α\le 1$. Here $κ_σ$ denotes the anisotropic curvature associated with a prescribed anisotropy function $σ$. We prove that every finite-time blow-up is of Type-II provided that the Euclidean length of the evolving curve remains uniformly bounded away from zero. This result holds without any symmetry assumptions on either the initial curve or the anisotropy. When the initial signed area is nonzero, this length lower bound is automatically satisfied, and thus any finite-time singularity is necessarily of Type-II. By means of Hamilton's rescaling method combined with a differential Harnack estimate, we show that along a suitable blow-up subsequence, the rescaled curves at the selected times converge locally to the profile of a translating solution of the corresponding anisotropic curvature flow $V=κ_σ^α$. We also establish sufficient conditions for finite-time singularity formation in terms of the initial signed area and anisotropic length. Moreover, we construct locally convex initial curves arbitrarily close to the Wulff curve for which a finite-time Type-II singularity occurs.

</details>


### [99] [The divergence of continuously differentiable vector fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35452)
*Thierry De Pauw*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper fully characterizes continuous functions on R^m (m≥2) that are divergences of C^1 vector fields, using distributions and the dual of a subspace of the Arens–Eells space, with two contrasting applications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding which continuous scalar functions can be written as the divergence of a continuously differentiable vector field is a natural but not fully settled question for dimensions m≥2; a complete characterization was lacking.

Method: The authors formulate a complete characterization of such functions in terms of distributions, and equivalently via the continuous dual of a suitably topologised subspace of the Arens–Eells space.

Result: Two contrasting applications follow: (1) a compactly supported continuous function exists that is the divergence of a bounded Lipschitz vector field but of no C^1 vector field; (2) every continuous function whose distributional partial derivatives are locally m-integrable (the Sobolev critical exponent) is the divergence of a C^1 vector field.

Conclusion: The characterization provides a sharp and complete picture of divergence-representability of continuous functions, revealing both obstructions (Lipschitz vs. C^1) and a positive critical-exponent Sobolev-type result.

Abstract: For $m\geq 2$, we give a complete characterisation of the continuous functions on $\mathbb{R}^m$ that are divergences of continuously differentiable vector fields. The characterisation is formulated in terms of distributions and, equivalently, in terms of the continuous dual of a suitably topologised subspace of the Arens--Eells space. The two implications yield contrasting applications. First, there exists a compactly supported continuous function which is the divergence of a bounded Lipschitz vector field but not of any continuously differentiable vector field. Second, every continuous function whose distributional partial derivatives are locally $m$-integrable (the critical exponent for Sobolev embedding) is the divergence of a continuously differentiable vector field.

</details>


### [100] [Extremal Trudinger-Moser inequality associated with many point vortices with multi-intensities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35471)
*Takashi Suzuki,Yohei Toyota*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies boundedness of an extremal 2D Trudinger–Moser inequality arising from many point vortices with multiple intensities, and provides sufficient conditions under which continuously-distributed measures keep the extremal functional bounded.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Point-vortex systems with several intensity classes lead to extremal Trudinger–Moser-type inequalities whose boundedness is not immediate; understanding when these extremals stay bounded is needed to make the associated variational/mean-field analysis well-posed.

Method: Analysis of the extremal 2D Trudinger–Moser inequality for multi-intensity point vortices, deriving and verifying sufficient conditions on continuously-distributed measures, treated separately in one-sided and two-sided cases.

Result: Sufficient conditions are established for continuously-distributed measures, in both the one-sided and two-sided settings, guaranteeing boundedness of the extremal inequality.

Conclusion: Boundedness of the multi-intensity vortex Trudinger–Moser extremal problem holds under explicit sufficient conditions on the measure, split into one-sided and two-sided regimes.

Abstract: We study the boundedness of extremal 2D Trudinger-Moser inequality associated with many point vortices with multi-intensities. Sufficient conditions for continuously-distributed measures are given in one-sided and two-sided cases.

</details>


### [101] [Differentiability and Convexity of the Solution Maps for Fully Non-linear Parabolic HJB Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35476)
*Jean-François Chassagneux,Stéphane Crépey,Grégoire Loeper,Justin Ruelland*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies how solutions of a fully nonlinear parabolic HJB equation depend on a finite-dimensional terminal condition, proving continuous directional differentiability and local strong convexity of the point-evaluated solution map, and extends these results to cascades of Cauchy problems coupled through terminal conditions at several times.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivated by a dual formulation of semimartingale optimal transport, where one needs to understand how solutions vary with the terminal condition along a finite-dimensional family; the cascade extension is driven by optimal transport problems with marginal constraints at several maturities.

Method: Formulates a terminal-value Cauchy problem on an unbounded spatial domain under structural ellipticity and regularity assumptions. Reviews local parabolic Hölder estimates and stability results for bounded uniformly continuous terminal data; establishes continuous directional differentiability of the solution map via the linearized Cauchy problem; uses Evans--Krylov theory for fully nonlinear parabolic PDEs combined with probabilistic representations. Under Hamiltonian convexity and terminal-data non-degeneracy, proves local strong convexity.

Result: The solution map is continuously directionally differentiable with derivative identified by the linearized Cauchy problem. Under additional convexity of the Hamiltonian and a non-degeneracy condition on terminal data, the point-evaluated solution map is locally strongly convex. These differentiability and convexity results extend to cascades of coupled Cauchy problems with terminal conditions at several prescribed times.

Conclusion: The paper provides a rigorous differential and convexity analysis of HJB solution maps with respect to finite-dimensional terminal conditions, supporting dual approaches to semimartingale optimal transport with multi-marginal constraints, via a combination of Evans--Krylov PDE theory and probabilistic methods.

Abstract: We study the dependence on the terminal condition of solutions to a fully nonlinear parabolic Hamilton--Jacobi--Bellman (HJB) equation, when the terminal condition varies along a finite-dimensional family. This problem is motivated by a dual formulation of semimartingale optimal transport. The equation is posed as a terminal-value Cauchy problem on an unbounded spatial domain. Under structural ellipticity and regularity assumptions on the nonlinear operator, we recall local parabolic Hölder estimates and stability results for bounded uniformly continuous terminal data. We then establish continuous directional differentiability of the solution map with respect to the finite-dimensional parameter, and identify its derivative through the corresponding linearized Cauchy problem. Under an additional convexity assumption on the Hamiltonian and a non-degeneracy condition on the terminal data, we prove local strong convexity of the point-evaluated solution map. The analysis relies on Evans--Krylov theory for fully nonlinear parabolic equations and combines PDE arguments with probabilistic representations. Finally, we extend the differentiability and convexity results to cascades of Cauchy problems in which successive equations are coupled through terminal conditions at several prescribed times. This structure is motivated, for instance, by optimal transport problems with marginal constraints imposed at several maturities.

</details>


### [102] [A Liouville Theorem for the Real k-Hessian Equation with a One-Sided Growth Condition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35565)
*Jingchen Hu,Heming Jiao*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The authors prove a scale-invariant interior $C^2$ estimate for the real $k$-Hessian equation and use it to show that any smooth entire solution of $\sigma_k(D^2u)=1$ with quadratic growth lower bound $u(x)\ge b|x|^2-B$ ($b>0$) must be a quadratic polynomial.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Entire solutions of the $k$-Hessian equation $\sigma_k(D^2u)=1$ and their rigidity are of interest in fully nonlinear PDE theory. A key difficulty is the lack of a suitable scale-invariant interior $C^2$ estimate, which is needed to control the behavior of solutions globally from a one-sided growth condition.

Method: They establish a scale-invariant interior $C^2$ estimate for smooth solutions of the real $k$-Hessian equation. This estimate is then applied to entire solutions satisfying the lower quadratic growth bound, and the argument extends to viscosity solutions and to weak solutions in Trudinger's sense (where admissibility must be assumed).

Result: Every smooth entire solution of $\sigma_k(D^2u)=1$ obeying $u(x)\ge b|x|^2-B$ with $b>0$ is a quadratic polynomial. For smooth solutions, admissibility is automatically forced by the growth condition. The conclusions also hold for viscosity solutions and for weak solutions in Trudinger's sense, with admissibility required in the latter case.

Conclusion: The scale-invariant interior $C^2$ estimate serves as a powerful tool yielding a rigidity theorem: entire $k$-Hessian solutions with quadratic lower growth are quadratic polynomials. The result is robust, extending across smooth, viscosity, and Trudinger weak solution frameworks.

Abstract: We establish a scale-invariant interior $C^2$ estimate for smooth solutions of the real $k$-Hessian equation. As an application, we prove that every smooth entire solution of $σ_k(D^2u)=1$ satisfying $u(x)\ge b|x|^2-B$ for some constants $b,B$ with $b>0$ is a quadratic polynomial. For smooth solutions, admissibility is forced by the growth condition.
  The results also hold for viscosity solutions and for weak solutions in Trudinger's sense. Admissibility is required in the latter case.

</details>


### [103] [Gradient estimates and Liouville theorems for exponentially harmonic functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35571)
*Xishen Jin,Xi Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper derives oscillation-based local and one-sided pointwise gradient estimates for exponentially harmonic functions on complete manifolds with Ric ≥ -K g, yielding a Liouville theorem on Ricci-nonnegative manifolds under one-sided sublinear growth.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Exponentially harmonic functions satisfy a quasilinear PDE that generalizes harmonic functions; gradient estimates and Liouville-type rigidity results are well developed for harmonic functions but less so for this nonlinear counterpart, especially under one-sided boundedness rather than global two-sided control.

Method: Work on complete Riemannian manifolds with a lower Ricci curvature bound Ric_g ≥ -K g. Establish a local gradient estimate for exponentially harmonic functions expressed in terms of the oscillation of the function, then upgrade it to a pointwise gradient estimate for solutions bounded from one side (above or below).

Result: A local oscillation-type gradient estimate and a one-sided pointwise gradient estimate are proven. As an application, on Ric_g ≥ 0 manifolds a Liouville theorem holds under a one-sided sublinear growth condition; in particular any exponentially harmonic function bounded above or bounded below must be constant.

Conclusion: Gradient estimates for exponentially harmonic functions extend to one-sided bounded settings, giving new Liouville rigidity; the key technique is controlling the gradient via oscillation rather than full bounds.

Abstract: We study exponentially harmonic functions on complete Riemannian manifolds. Under a lower Ricci curvature bound \(\Ric_g\geq-Kg\), we first establish a local gradient estimate in terms of the oscillation. We then prove a pointwise gradient estimate for solutions bounded from one side. As an application, when \(\Ric_g\geq0\), we obtain a Liouville theorem under a one-sided sublinear growth condition. In particular, every exponentially harmonic function bounded above or below is constant.

</details>


### [104] [Asymptotic observable symmetry for spacetime observability of waves II: Smooth bounded domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35610)
*Jingrui Niu,Shengquan Xiang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper extends prior work on symmetry of spacetime observability for wave equations, proving that measuring over a fixed measurable set satisfying the essential spacetime measurable geometric control condition is insufficient to yield observability, due to effects of glancing propagation and caustics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior work (NWX) and Part I (AOSI) introduced asymptotic observable symmetry (AOS) for spacetime observability of wave equations. The remaining question is how symmetry behaves under harder geometric scenarios—glancing propagation and caustics—where standard geometric control heuristics may break down.

Method: The authors build on the asymptotic observable symmetry framework from Part I and analyze wave equations on smooth bounded domains in R^2. They construct, for every positive observation time, a fixed measurable set in spacetime and examine its geometric control properties under glancing rays and caustic formation.

Result: On every smooth bounded domain in R^2 and for every positive observation time, there exists a fixed measurable set satisfying the essential spacetime measurable geometric control condition; yet the observability inequality fails on this set.

Conclusion: Satisfying the (measurable) geometric control condition is not sufficient for spacetime observability when glancing propagation and caustics are present, revealing a gap between geometric control conditions and actual observability—an instance of symmetry breaking in the AOS framework.

Abstract: Continuing \cite{NWX} and Part I \cite{AOSI}, we further investigate symmetry for spacetime observability of wave equations. Building on asymptotic observable symmetry (AOS) introduced in \cite{AOSI}, we study symmetry under glancing propagation and caustics. We prove that, on every smooth bounded domain in $\R^2$ and for every positive observation time, there is a fixed measurable set satisfying the essential spacetime measurable geometric control condition. However, the observability inequality fails on this set.

</details>


### [105] [Sharp volume tests and entropy thresholds for quasilinear elliptic inequalities with gradient reactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35688)
*Yongheng Qin,Yuhua Sun,Jie Xiao*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies positive weak solutions of the nonlinear inequality −Δ_m u ≥ u^p|∇u|^q on complete noncompact Riemannian manifolds, deriving curvature-free integral volume tests and sharp thresholds without assuming curvature, volume doubling, or Poincaré inequalities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing Liouville-type results and region classifications for such mixed reaction-diffusion inequalities typically rely on curvature or volume hypotheses. The authors aim to refine volume conditions, remove curvature assumptions, and establish sharpness within model families.

Method: Combining two first-order estimates with a recurrence for the mass of a fixed density, the authors build a curvature-free integral volume test. This organizes pure-gradient and capacity criteria and gives finite hierarchies of iterated-logarithmic growth bounds, with counterexamples on smooth model manifolds. A vanishing lower-growth condition on log V(R)/R^{m−q} yields a Liouville property in the subhomogeneous region.

Result: The work yields sharp finite hierarchies of iterated-logarithmic growth bounds and counterexamples on model manifolds. On the homogeneous line p+q=m−1, it gives the optimal strict lower-volume-entropy threshold (p+1)((m−1)/p)^{p/(p+1)} for p>0 (and 1 for p=0), with examples achieving equality. The tests remain valid for smooth weighted measures and locally bounded positive Sobolev solutions.

Conclusion: The advances concern refined volume hypotheses and their model sharpness rather than a new region classification. Sharpness is understood only within the stated model families; no characterization from volume alone on individual manifolds is claimed.

Abstract: We investigate positive weak solutions of $-Δ_m u\ge u^p|\nabla u|^q$ on complete noncompact Riemannian manifolds without curvature, volume doubling, or Poincaré assumptions. For the superhomogeneous mixed reaction we derive a curvature-free integral volume test by combining two first-order estimates with a recurrence for the mass of a \emph{fixed} density. The same approach organizes the pure-gradient and capacity criteria and yields sharp finite hierarchies of iterated-logarithmic growth bounds, with counterexamples on smooth model manifolds. In the subhomogeneous region, vanishing lower growth of $\log V(R)/R^{m-q}$ forces the Liouville property. On the homogeneous line $p+q=m-1$, we obtain the optimal strict lower-volume-entropy threshold $(p+1)((m-1)/p)^{p/(p+1)}$ for $p>0$ (and $1$ for $p=0$), together with examples at equality. The existing six-region classification is not claimed as new; the advances concern refined volume hypotheses and their model sharpness. The tests remain valid for smooth weighted measures and for locally bounded positive Sobolev solutions. Sharpness is understood within the stated model families; no characterization from volume alone on an individual manifold is asserted.

</details>


### [106] [Unconditional scattering below the ground state for the three-dimensional focusing energy-critical generalized Hartree equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35700)
*Pang-hung Chung,Dan Han*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves unconditional global existence and scattering for the 3D focusing energy-critical generalized Hartree equation with 0<α<3, assuming initial data in Ḣ¹ below the ground-state energy and kinetic thresholds, without radial, finite-mass, or finite-variance conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior global existence and scattering results for this energy-critical Hartree model typically required extra assumptions such as radial symmetry, finite mass, or finite variance. The authors aim to remove these restrictions and establish unconditional results using only Ḣ¹ regularity below the sharp thresholds.

Method: The proof proceeds by contradiction: annular (frequency-localized) estimates together with Gaussian interaction estimates are used to rule out the existence of a compact critical (blow-up) trajectory. This is done by first controlling the nonlinear forcing term and then recovering the conserved mass of the solution.

Result: Global existence and scattering are obtained unconditionally for all Ḣ¹ data below the ground-state energy and kinetic thresholds, with no symmetry, finite-mass, or finite-variance hypotheses.

Conclusion: The threshold restrictions (radial symmetry, finite mass, finite variance) can be removed for the 3D focusing energy-critical generalized Hartree equation via the annular/Gaussian estimate approach, yielding unconditional global well-posedness and scattering below the sharp thresholds.

Abstract: Unconditional global existence and scattering are established for the three-dimensional focusing energy-critical generalized Hartree equation with $0<α<3$, below the ground-state energy and kinetic thresholds. The initial data lie in $\dot H^1$, with no radial symmetry, finite-mass, or finite-variance assumption. Annular frequency estimates and Gaussian interaction estimates rule out a compact critical trajectory by first controlling its nonlinear forcing and then recovering its conserved mass.

</details>


### [107] [Existence and uniqueness of solutions for a random PDE-ODE model describing glioma invasion and radiotherapy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35763)
*Martina Conte,Sandesh Athni Hiremath,Christina Surulescu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves existence and uniqueness of local strong solutions for a stochastic PDE-ODE model of glioma growth and invasion in anisotropic brain tissue, including random radiotherapy effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Glioma invasion models need to account for anisotropy of brain tissue and uncertainties of radiotherapeutic interventions; a rigorous stochastic continuum foundation is still missing.

Method: A macroscopically upscaled (from a microscopic description) coupled nonlinear PDE-ODE system combining glioma and healthy tissue dynamics, anisotropic diffusion, taxis mechanisms, and stochastic depletion terms; analysis establishes local strong well-posedness.

Result: Existence and uniqueness of local strong solutions to the stochastic system are proven despite nonlinear coupling, anisotropic diffusion, taxis, and stochastic treatment effects.

Conclusion: The work provides a first rigorous mathematical step toward understanding stochastic continuum models of glioma invasion and therapy response; global well-posedness remains open.

Abstract: We study a stochastic model for the growth and invasion of glioma cells in the anisotropic brain tissue, also incorporating effects of radiotherapeutic interventions and associated uncertainties. The PDE-ODE model couples glioma and healthy tissue dynamics in a nonlinear manner and explicitly includes treatment-related stochasticity through random depletion terms. The macroscopic system has been obtained by an upscaling procedure from a microscopic description in [1]. We prove the existence and uniqueness of local strong solutions to the corresponding stochastic system. The analysis addresses challenges arising from the nonlinear coupling, anisotropic diffusion, taxis mechanisms, and stochastic treatment effects, providing a first rigorous step toward the mathematical understanding of stochastic continuum models for glioma invasion and therapy response.

</details>


### [108] [Traveling Waves for Mixed Nonlocal-Local Systems of Conservation Laws on $\mathbb{S}^1$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35771)
*Rinaldo M. Colombo,Mauro Garavello,Claudia Nocita*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Genuinely nonlinear conservation laws rarely admit nontrivial traveling waves, and scalar nonlocal ones often share this limitation. This paper shows mixed nonlocal-local systems of conservation laws are far richer: multiple traveling waves can exist, components may travel at different speeds, and traveling waves can emerge as time-asymptotic limits. The results require a substantial extension of Dafermos' "trend to steady state" theory via Hamiltonian systems solved by generalized characteristics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Nontrivial traveling waves hardly appear in genuinely nonlinear conservation laws, and in some cases scalar nonlocal conservation laws inherit this scarcity. The authors aim to show that mixed nonlocal-local systems break this restrictive pattern and exhibit a much richer wave structure.

Method: The analysis extends Dafermos' "trend to steady state" theory significantly. It exploits properties of Hamiltonian systems that are solved by generalized characteristics, applied to mixed nonlocal-local systems of conservation laws.

Result: A variety of traveling waves may exist in mixed nonlocal-local systems. Different components may travel with different speeds, and traveling waves can appear as time-asymptotic limits.

Conclusion: Mixed nonlocal-local conservation law systems admit a far richer traveling-wave scenario than purely local or scalar nonlocal models, with multiple waves, differing component speeds, and asymptotic emergence of traveling waves. Establishing this requires extending Dafermos' trend-to-steady-state framework using Hamiltonian system properties and generalized characteristics.

Abstract: Non trivial traveling waves in genuinely nonlinear conservation laws hardly appear and, in some cases, scalar nonlocal conservation laws inherit this feature. Here, we show that mixed nonlocal-local systems of conservation laws display a far richer scenario. A variety of traveling waves may exist, different components may travel with different speeds and traveling waves can appear as time-asymptotic limits. Technically, these results need a significant extension of Dafermos' "trend to steady state" theory, exploiting properties of Hamiltonian systems solved by generalized characteristics.

</details>


### [109] [On the Nash equilibrium for a model of traffic flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35772)
*Alberto Bressan,Daniel Lee Harmon,Elio Marconi*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Study of Nash equilibria in a conservation-law model of traffic flow where drivers choose optimal departure times. The arrival-time map is shown Hölder (1/2) in Lagrangian and Lipschitz in Eulerian variables; Nash equilibria generically contain a shock (a queue forms), and an integro-differential evolution of departure rates admits a unique global solution, supporting earlier evidence of instability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide rigorous analytical foundations for a conservation-law model of departure-time choice, understanding the regularity and structure of Nash equilibria and the dynamics when departure rates are off-equilibrium (as observed in numerical simulations showing instability and possible chaos).

Method: Consider a scalar conservation law for highway traffic; each driver selects a departure time minimizing a cost combining departure cost φ(τ^d) and arrival cost ψ(τ^a). Analyze the map from departure distribution to arrival time with L^1 continuity estimates (Lagrangian/Eulerian). Investigate regularity of Nash equilibria for generic C^2 costs, and study a nonlocal integro-differential evolution equation for the departure rate.

Result: The arrival-time map is Hölder continuous with exponent 1/2 in Lagrangian variables and Lipschitz continuous in Eulerian variables. For sufficiently many drivers, a queue always forms at the entrance and the traffic density contains a shock; for any number of drivers and generic C^2 cost functions, the Nash equilibrium still contains a shock. The departure-rate evolution equation admits a unique global-in-time solution.

Conclusion: Nash equilibria in this traffic model are structurally singular (shock/queue formation) rather than smooth, and off-equilibrium departure-rate dynamics are well-posed globally, giving theoretical support to earlier numerical indications of instability and possible chaotic behavior.

Abstract: The paper consider a conservation law model of traffic flow on a highway, where each driver chooses an optimal departure time $τ^d$, based on a departure cost $\varphi(τ^d)$ and an arrival cost $ψ(τ^a)$. Nash equilibria are studied, where no driver can reduce his own cost by changing his departure time.
  The first results provide continuity estimates on the arrival time, depending on the distribution of departures. In terms of the ${\bf L}^1$ distance, we show that the corresponding map is Hölder continuous with exponent $1/2$ in Lagrangian variables (labelling individual drivers), and Lipschitz continuous in Eulerian space variables.
  Concerning the regularity of the a Nash equilibrium, our results show that, when the total number of drivers is sufficiently large, a queue alway forms at the entrance of the highway and the corresponding traffic density contains a shock. Moreover, for any number of drivers (possibly small), we prove that for generic $\mathcal C^2$ cost functions $\varphi, ψ$ the Nash equilibrium solution still contains a shock.
  In the last section of the paper, assuming that the initial departure rate is not a Nash equilibrium, we consider an evolution equation for the departure rate, where each driver keeps changing his daily departure time in order to reduce his own cost. It is proved that this nonlocal integro-differential evolution equation admits a unique solution, globally in time. This provides some theoretical support to earlier numerical simulations, which indicated the dynamic instability of the Nash equilibrium and the possible existence of a chaotic attractor.

</details>


### [110] [The generic structure of the singular set in the Stefan problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35780)
*Giacomo Colombo,Alessio Figalli*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: For generic solutions of the Stefan problem in any dimension $n\ge2$, the singular set Σ is countably parabolically $(n-2)$-rectifiable, so its parabolic dimension is at most $n-2$; the key sharp ingredient is that the maximal stratum of a generic solution consists of discrete points in space-time.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Stefan problem (phase-change/free-boundary problem) admits solutions whose singular set can be complex; understanding the size and structure of the singular set of generic solutions is a basic regularity question left open in general dimension.

Method: A Baire-category genericity argument: prove a sharp structural theorem that, in every space dimension, points in the maximal stratum of a generic solution are discrete in space-time, then bootstrap this discreteness via a stratification of the singular set to obtain parabolic rectifiability.

Result: In every space dimension $n\ge2$, the singular set Σ of a generic solution is countably parabolically $(n-2)$-rectifiable, hence $\dim_{\mathrm{par}}(\Sigma)\le n-2$; the discreteness of the maximal stratum holds sharply in all dimensions.

Conclusion: Generic Stefan-problem solutions have singular sets of optimal parabolic codimension at least 2, with an $(n-2)$-rectifiable structure, unifying all dimensions $n\ge2$.

Abstract: We show that, in every space dimension $n\ge2$, the singular set $Σ$ of a generic (in the Baire-category sense) solution of the Stefan problem is countably parabolically $(n-2)$-rectifiable. In particular, $\dim_{\mathrm{par}}(Σ)\le n-2$. This is achieved by proving the following sharp result: in every space dimension, points of the maximal stratum of a generic solution are discrete in space-time.

</details>


### [111] [Parabolic Rectifiable Reifenberg for Measures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35809)
*Robert Koirala*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper develops a structure theory for Radon measures on R^n × R with the parabolic distance, establishing rectifiable Reifenberg theorems, Jones square function characterizations of regular parabolic rectifiability, and sharp L^p beta number results, all formalized in Lean 4 with Mathlib.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing structure theories (Edelen–Naber–Valtorta, Azzam–Tolsa, David–Semmes) address rectifiability in Euclidean settings but not the space-time geometry of parabolic distance, motivating an extension to parabolic dimensions.

Method: A structure theory for measures on R^n × R under the parabolic distance, including a parabolic rectifiable Reifenberg theorem for arbitrary Radon measures, qualitative/quantitative characterizations via Jones square functions, and sharp L^p beta number results; proofs are formalized in Lean 4 with Mathlib.

Result: Parabolic analogues of Reifenberg-type rectifiability for arbitrary Radon measures, Jones square function characterizations of regular parabolic rectifiability, and sharp L^p beta number results, valid in every parabolic dimension.

Conclusion: The theory extends prior Euclidean rectifiability results to space-time, and the theorems and corollaries are formally verified in Lean 4, demonstrating feasibility of machine-checked geometric measure theory.

Abstract: We announce a structure theory for measures on $\mathbb{R}^n\times\mathbb{R}$ equipped with the parabolic distance. Our results include a parabolic rectifiable Reifenberg theorem for arbitrary Radon measures, qualitative and quantitative characterizations of regular parabolic rectifiability by Jones square functions, and sharp results for $L^p$ beta numbers. The results hold in every parabolic dimension and extend the theories of Edelen--Naber--Valtorta, Azzam--Tolsa and David--Semmes to space-time. The stated theorems and corollaries, together with their proofs, are formalized in Lean~4 with Mathlib.

</details>


### [112] [Characteristic gluing to extremal Kerr horizons and the third law of black hole thermodynamics in vacuum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35836)
*Stefan Czimek*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The authors prove a non-perturbative characteristic gluing theorem showing that any Boyer-Lindquist sphere of a Kerr black hole (including every Schwarzschild symmetry sphere) can be glued along a single outgoing null hypersurface to an extremal Kerr horizon sphere of large enough mass, exactly matching full sphere data. They apply this to build asymptotically flat vacuum initial data whose development contains an exactly Schwarzschild apparent horizon and later an exactly extremal Kerr event horizon—the first rigorous vacuum counterexample to the third law of black hole thermodynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Kehle and Unger conjectured (in the extremal case) that characteristic gluing to extremal horizons should be possible non-perturbatively with full sphere data; the paper resolves this and, as a consequence, tests the third law of black hole thermodynamics. The third law was previously only established in restricted settings or perturbatively, leaving open whether extremal horizons can form from regular initial data in vacuum.

Method: A constructive characteristic gluing argument along a single outgoing null hypersurface. First, angular momentum is raised to the extremal value by shearing pulses of non-axisymmetric gravitational radiation. Then incoming data are damped exponentially along a long non-expanding neck. At the degenerate target horizon the linearised problem is inverted at all angular frequencies via a factorisation of a fourth-order angular operator into an angular Tealpoti–Starobinsky product.

Result: An exact, non-perturbative, non-axisymmetric characteristic gluing theorem for the Einstein vacuum equations, gluing Kerr/Schwarzschild spheres to extremal Kerr horizon spheres of sufficiently large mass, exact at both ends and matching full sphere data including transversal curvature. As an application, one-ended asymptotically flat vacuum initial data are constructed whose maximal development contains an exactly Schwarzschild apparent horizon and, at a later advanced time, an exactly extremal Kerr event horizon.

Conclusion: The paper proves Kehle and Unger's extremal-case gluing conjecture and, via the constructed initial data, provides the first rigorous counterexample to the third law of black hole thermodynamics in vacuum, also confirming a second Kehle–Unger conjecture. This shows extremal horizons can arise in maximal developments of regular vacuum data.

Abstract: We prove a non-perturbative, non-axisymmetric characteristic gluing theorem for the Einstein vacuum equations: every Boyer-Lindquist sphere in the domain of outer communication of a Kerr black hole, in particular every symmetry sphere of a Schwarzschild exterior, can be glued along a single outgoing null hypersurface to a horizon sphere of an extremal Kerr black hole of sufficiently large mass. The gluing is exact at both ends and matches the full sphere data, including the transversal curvature. This proves a conjecture of Kehle and Unger in the extremal case. We first raise the angular momentum to the extremal value by rotating shear pulses of non-axisymmetric gravitational radiation, and then damp the incoming data exponentially along a long non-expanding neck. At the degenerate target horizon we invert the linearised problem at all angular frequencies, using a factorisation of a fourth-order angular operator into an angular Teukolsky-Starobinsky product.
  As an application, we construct one-ended asymptotically flat vacuum initial data whose maximal development contains an exactly Schwarzschild apparent horizon and, at a later advanced time, an exactly extremal Kerr event horizon. This is the first rigorous counterexample to the third law of black hole thermodynamics in vacuum, and proves a second conjecture of Kehle and Unger.

</details>


### [113] [$C^{1,α}$ and $W^{2,1+\varepsilon}$ regularity for the $2$-Hessian equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35839)
*Yasheng Lyu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves interior and global a priori C^{1,α} estimates (for some α∈(0,1)) for 2-admissible solutions of the 2-Hessian equation, derived from W^{2,1+ε} bounds, plus global W^{2,1+ε} estimates under strictly mean convex C^{2,β} domains and boundary values, showing C^{2,β} assumptions are optimal.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To upgrade W^{2,1+ε} regularity to C^{1,α} regularity for the 2-Hessian equation and to establish global W^{2,1+ε} estimates, filling a regularity gap in the Hölder scale for this fully nonlinear equation.

Method: A priori analysis for bounded smooth 2-admissible solutions: interior C^{1,α} estimates requiring only an upper density bound, obtained by combining W^{2,1+ε} bounds with a slight modification of an existing interior W^{2,1+ε} argument; global estimates use positive upper and lower density bounds on bounded strictly mean convex C^{2,β} domains with C^{2,β} boundary values.

Result: Interior and global C^{1,α} estimates for some α∈(0,1) from W^{2,1+ε} bounds, and global W^{2,1+ε} estimates for some ε>0 on strictly mean convex C^{2,β} domains. Known examples show the C^{2,β} regularity of boundary and boundary values is optimal in the Hölder scale for both the global W^{2,1+ε} and global C^{1,α} estimates.

Conclusion: The 2-Hessian equation admits C^{1,α} estimates derived from W^{2,1+ε} data under mild density assumptions, and the C^{2,β} boundary regularity assumptions are sharp in the Hölder scale.

Abstract: In this paper, for every $\varepsilon>0$, we establish interior and global a priori $C^{1,α}$ estimates for some $α\in(0,1)$ from $W^{2,1+\varepsilon}$ bounds for bounded smooth $2$-admissible solutions of the $2$-Hessian equation, with the interior estimate requiring only an upper bound on the density. Under positive upper and lower density bounds, we prove global $W^{2,1+\varepsilon}$ estimates for some $\varepsilon>0$ on bounded strictly mean convex $C^{2,β}$ domains with $C^{2,β}$ boundary values. For densities bounded above and below by positive constants, the interior $C^{1,α}$ estimate follows by combining the first result with an interior $W^{2,1+\varepsilon}$ estimate obtained by a slight modification of an existing argument. Combining the first result with the global $W^{2,1+\varepsilon}$ estimate yields the global $C^{1,α}$ estimate. Known examples show that the $C^{2,β}$ regularity assumptions on the boundary and boundary values are optimal in the Hölder scale for both the global $W^{2,1+\varepsilon}$ estimate and the global $C^{1,α}$ estimate.

</details>


<div id='physics.comp-ph'></div>

### [114] [An Efficient Quantum Spectral-Galerkin Method for Linear Elliptic Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33095)
*Dongjie Wang,Sheng Chen*

Main category: physics.comp-ph

TL;DR: An efficient quantum spectral-Galerkin method for constant-coefficient second-order linear elliptic equations using a boundary-adapted Jacobi tensor-product basis, achieving poly(d, log(1/ε)) state preparation and functional estimation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Fixed-order finite-difference and finite-element discretizations yield poor precision dependence for quantum linear solver algorithms on elliptic PDEs; the authors aim to improve the ε-dependence of solution state preparation.

Method: A spectral-Galerkin discretization with a boundary-adapted Jacobi tensor-product basis that enforces boundary conditions within the approximation space, producing Galerkin systems with polynomially bounded condition numbers and sparsity; analytic solutions under a uniform regularity condition enable spectral convergence, and linear functionals are estimated via norm and inner-product estimation.

Result: For analytic solutions satisfying a uniform regularity condition, the normalized Galerkin coefficient state can be prepared with complexity poly(d, log(1/ε)) under standard sparse-access and state-preparation assumptions, improving precision dependence over fixed-order finite-difference/finite-element discretizations; functional estimation incurs an additional O(1/ε) factor.
