DW reproduces previously published classical coefficients and the classical drift-space limit, while FB gives a different result. Both are identical for zero injection velocity. ES-PIC comparisons show FB agrees better for higher injection energy at smaller W/D, and DW better at larger W/D.

Conclusion: A weakly relativistic SCLC formulation with arbitrary injection energy is established and validated. For finite emission area, the choice between FB and DW models depends on injection energy and W/D aspect ratio, providing guidance for selecting appropriate 2D corrections.

Abstract: We develop a weakly relativistic and electrostatic formulation of space-charge-limited current (SCLC) with arbitrary injection energy, covering the accelerating-diode and drift-space regimes. The one-dimensional (1D) relativistic SCLC model is obtained from a stationary-current condition, which is validated against the numerical solutions. Based on this relativistic 1D SCLC model, we investigate two different approaches to account for the effects of finite emission area for a two-dimensional (2D) planar diode of a finite width of $W$, which is larger than the gap spacing $D$. The field-balance (FB) approach evaluates the opposing space-charge fields at the potential minimum, whereas the density-weighted (DW) approach uses the mean absolute distance of electrons from that minimum. The two approaches yield different corrections at finite injection energy, even in the non-relativistic limit, with FB model predicting a larger leading-order enhancement. DW model reproduces our previously published classical coefficient and the classical drift-space limit, whereas the FB model gives a different result. Note both approaches are identical for zero injection velocity. Comparisons with 2D electrostatic particle-in-cell (ES-PIC) simulations, it is found that FB and DW model show better agreement for higher injection energy (= gap voltage) at smaller and larger $W/D$ emission area respectively.

</details>


<div id='cs.SI'></div>

# cs.SI [[Back]](#toc)

### [140] [Resource competition and survival for random walkers on complex networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33831)
*Snehal Shekatkar*

Main category: cs.SI

TL;DR: The paper studies survival time of W random walkers consuming resource on complex networks, showing topology and resource distribution effects and that biasing movement toward low-degree vertices extends survival on scale-free networks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding how network topology and resource production statistics affect the survival of resource-consuming random walkers, a model relevant to foraging, search, and dynamic processes on networks.

Method: Numerical simulations of W walkers on Erdős-Rényi and scale-free networks with stochastic resource production (uniform vs. exponential, equal mean); an exact expression for the survival-time distribution assuming independent walker lifespans; and experiments with degree-biased walker movement.

Result: Erdős-Rényi networks give higher mean survival time than scale-free networks; uniform resource production gives higher survival time than exponential with equal mean; the independent-lifespan formula reveals strong lifespan dependence under scale-free topology but not homogeneous topology; biasing movement toward low-degree vertices substantially increases survival on scale-free but not Erdős-Rényi networks.

Conclusion: Survival time is jointly governed by topology and resource distribution, and can be improved via degree-biased movement on scale-free networks without changing resource production or topology.

Abstract: We consider the problem of $W$ random walkers on a complex network where each walker requires an amount $θ$ of resource at each time to survive. Resource is stochastically produced at each vertex of the network with a specified distribution. The survival time of the system of walkers is defined as the time required for all $W$ walkers to die. Using numerical simulations, we show that Erd{\H o}s-Rényi networks a have higher mean survival time than Scale-free networks as expected, but also that uniform distribution production leads to higher mean survival time than exponential distribution with the same mean. We derive an exact expression for the survival-time distribution under the assumption of independent walker lifespans, and use it to show that scale-free network topology produces strong dependence in the lifespans but homogeneous topology does not. We moreover demonstrate how the survival time can be substantially increased without changing the resource production or network topology by appropriately biasing the movement of the walkers towards low degree vertices on Scale-free networks, but has no effect on Erd{\H o}s-R{é}nyi networks.

</details>


<div id='physics.acc-ph'></div>

# physics.acc-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [141] [Maximization of accelerated charge in multibunch-driven plasma wakefield accelerators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35049)
*Konstantin Lotov*

Main category: physics.acc-ph

TL;DR: Using a train of driver bunches to drive the plasma wakefield can greatly boost accelerated charge if the witness bunches also form a train, but driver reshaping at high amplitudes complicates this. The authors propose an equilibrium train of initially inefficient driver bunches that becomes efficient once witness bunches are injected, demonstrated with a self-modulating driver beam.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Multi-bunch driver trains could significantly increase accelerated charge, since driver bunches replenish wave energy after witness passage, yielding comparable charges in both trains. However, at high wakefield amplitudes the driver bunches reshape as they reach radial equilibrium with the wave, and witness bunches cannot preserve quality during this process—yet they must load the wave to prevent amplitude growth that elongates the wakefield period and destroys the driver.

Method: Propose forming an equilibrium train of initially inefficient driver bunches that becomes efficient upon injection of the witness bunches; demonstrate the concept using a self-modulating driver beam, and identify factors favoring high witness charge.

Result: High witness charge is favored by: injecting additional "phaser" bunches at the zero-accelerating-field points, suppressing electron halo formation around the plasma, and operating at the highest possible wakefield amplitudes.

Conclusion: An equilibrium driver-bunch train that self-adjusts its efficiency upon witness injection offers a viable path to high-charge multi-bunch plasma wakefield acceleration, provided the beam loading, phaser injection, and halo suppression requirements are met at high amplitudes.

Abstract: Driving the plasma wakefield with a train of multiple particle bunches has the potential to significantly increase the accelerated charge, provided that the witness bunches also form a train. In this scenario, the driver bunches can replenish the wave energy after the passage of the witness bunches, yielding comparable charges in both trains. However, implementing this method at high wakefield amplitudes is complicated by the reshaping of the driver bunches as they reach radial equilibrium with the wave. The witness bunches cannot preserve their quality during this process; nevertheless, they must load the wave to prevent it from reaching the amplitude at which the wakefield period elongates and the wave destroys the driver. The proposed solution is to form an equilibrium train of initially inefficient driver bunches, which becomes efficient upon the injection of the witness bunches. This concept is demonstrated using the example of a self-modulating driver beam. Several factors favoring a high witness charge are identified: injecting additional ``phaser'' bunches at the zero-accelerating-field points, suppressing the formation of an electron halo around the plasma, and operating at the highest possible wakefield amplitudes.

</details>


<div id='math.DG'></div>

# math.DG [[Back]](#toc)

### [142] [Price's law for scalar waves on 1+4 dimensional subextremal Myers-Perry spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33812)
*Pieter-Bart Peters*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: The authors prove that the Hamiltonian flow for Myers–Perry black holes in 1+4 dimensions is normally hyperbolically trapped across the full subextremal range, and use this to derive a sharp Price law (with explicit decay rate t^{-3}) for scalar waves under real mode stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Kerr spacetime in 1+3 dimensions is well studied, but its higher-dimensional generalization, the Myers–Perry spacetime with two spin parameters, needs rigorous understanding of wave decay (Price law behavior). Prior work lacked a proof covering the full subextremal regime.

Method: They study the Hamiltonian flow associated to the inverse metric g^{-1} and establish normal hyperbolic trapping of the trapped set for all subextremal parameters satisfying (|a|+|b|)^2 < 2m. This dynamical/geometric framework is combined with the assumption of real mode stability to control the wave equation.

Result: Normal hyperbolicity of the trapped set is proven throughout the full subextremal range. As an application, an explicit Price law is obtained: solutions φ decay as φ(t,x) = c t^{-3} + O(t^{-4+}), where the leading constant c is explicitly computable.

Conclusion: The trapped set of Myers–Perry spacetimes with two spins is normally hyperbolic across the entire subextremal range, yielding sharp late-time decay (Price law) for scalar waves with an explicit leading-order term, generalizing Kerr results to 1+4 dimensions.

Abstract: We consider Myers--Perry spacetimes $(M,g_{\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b},\mathfrak{m}})$ in $1+4$ dimensions with two spin parameters $(\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b})\in\mathbb{R}^2$, which are a direct generalization of the well known Kerr spacetime in $1+3$ dimensions. We prove that the Hamiltonian flow associated to $g_{\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b},\mathfrak{m}}^{-1}$ is normally hyperbolically trapped for the full subextremal range \[
  (|\mathbf{a}|+|\mathbf{b}|)^2<2\mathfrak{m}. \] As an application, we prove a Price law with explicit leading order terms for solutions $φ(t,x)$ of the scalar wave equation \[
  \Box_{g_{\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b},\mathfrak{m}}}φ=0 \] under the assumption of real mode stability. Our precise asymptotics imply that $φ(t,x)=ct^{-3}+\mathcal{O}(t^{-4+})$, where $c\in\mathbb{C}$ is an explicitly computable constant.

</details>


### [143] [Sharp gradient comparison and rigidity for semilinear equations on manifolds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34207)
*Linlin Sun*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: Sharp amplitude and gradient comparison results for positive solutions of semilinear elliptic equations on complete manifolds with Ricci lower bounds; equality characterizes hyperbolic space, with boundary curvature bounds and rigidity for certain conformal compactifications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend comparison/rigidity theory (hyperbolic-space characterization via gradient estimates) to semilinear elliptic equations and their positive mixtures, and to apply it to conformal compactifications defined by a source equation.

Method: Sharp pointwise amplitude and gradient comparison estimates under a Ricci lower bound; uniform local estimates independent of mixing coefficients and number of terms for positive mixtures of subcritical powers; analysis of conformal compactifications governed by a source equation.

Result: Sharp comparisons obtained; equality at a single point forces hyperbolic space with the radial solution; sharp boundary scalar-curvature bounds and hyperbolic rigidity for the compactification class; non-Einstein examples constructed whose normalization is stable under small compactly supported metric perturbations.

Conclusion: Ricci-curvature lower bounds yield sharp, equality-rigid comparison estimates for semilinear elliptic solutions, transferring to sharp boundary scalar-curvature bounds and rigidity, and indicating the phenomenon is not confined to Einstein metrics.

Abstract: We prove sharp amplitude and gradient comparisons for positive solutions of a class of semilinear elliptic equations on complete manifolds under a Ricci lower bound. Equality at one point characterizes hyperbolic space and the corresponding radial solution. The local estimates are uniform in the mixing coefficients and number of terms for positive mixtures of subcritical powers. For a class of conformal compactifications defined by a source equation, we obtain sharp boundary scalar-curvature bounds and hyperbolic rigidity. We also construct non-Einstein examples whose normalization persists under small compactly supported metric perturbations.

</details>


### [144] [Length bounds and rigidity for ancient curve shortening flow on warped product surfaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34274)
*Beomseok Goh,Taehun Lee*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: The paper studies ancient solutions to curve shortening flow on warped product surfaces. For closed embedded non-null-homotopic ancient solutions, it shows graphicality at all times is equivalent to a uniform length bound as t→-∞, both holding when length is o(√-t). Under a positivity condition on the linearized height equation, every bounded-length solution is a family of slices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how ancient solutions of curve shortening flow behave in warped product geometries, and to characterize when such solutions remain graphical and become slices, analogous to rigidity results in Euclidean/rotational settings.

Method: Analysis of closed embedded ancient solutions on warped product surfaces; equivalence of graphicality and uniform length bounds; backward convergence to a limiting circle from the length bound; slice rigidity via an L^2 estimate for the height perturbation, assuming a positivity condition on the linearized height equation.

Result: Graphicality at every time is equivalent to a uniform length bound as t→-∞, and both hold whenever length is o(√-t). Under the extra positivity condition, any bounded-length such solution is a family of slices; backward convergence to a limiting circle and slice rigidity are established.

Conclusion: Ancient non-null-homotopic curve shortening flow solutions on warped product surfaces are governed by a graphicality–length-bound equivalence; with a positivity condition, bounded-length solutions are rigid slices converging backward to a circle.

Abstract: We study ancient solutions to curve shortening flow on a class of warped product surfaces. For closed embedded ancient solutions whose curves are not null-homotopic, we prove that graphicality at every time is equivalent to a uniform length bound as $t\to-\infty$, and that these properties hold whenever the length is $o(\sqrt{-t})$. Under an additional positivity condition on the linearized height equation, every such solution with bounded length is a family of slices. We derive backward convergence to a limiting circle from the length bound and prove slice rigidity by an $L^2$ estimate for the height perturbation.

</details>


### [145] [Pseudo-Calabi Flow on Toric Variety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34340)
*Junhao Tian,Kai Zheng*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: The paper proves long-time existence of the pseudo-Calabi flow on compact toric varieties for T^n-invariant initial Kähler metrics, and shows exponential convergence to a cscK metric when the Mabuchi functional is proper modulo automorphisms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a flow-based (parabolic) proof that properness of the K-energy implies existence of a constant scalar curvature Kähler (cscK) metric, specifically in the toric setting.

Method: Study the pseudo-Calabi flow restricted to T^n-invariant Kähler metrics on a compact toric variety; prove long-time existence and analyze convergence governed by the Mabuchi (K-energy) functional modulo automorphisms.

Result: Long-time existence of the flow is established for any T^n-invariant initial Kähler metric. Under properness of the Mabuchi functional modulo automorphisms, the flow converges exponentially to a cscK metric.

Conclusion: Properness of the K-energy implies the existence of a cscK metric in the toric setting, yielding a flow-theoretic proof of this implication.

Abstract: In this paper, we prove the long time existence of the pseudo-Calabi flow on a compact toric variety for any $T^n$-invariant initial Kähler metric. If the Mabuchi functional is proper modulo automorphisms, the flow converges exponentially to a constant scalar curvature Kähler (cscK) metric. In particular, this gives a flow proof that properness of the K-energy implies the existence of a cscK metric in the toric setting.

</details>


### [146] [Genus Increase in Closed Mean Curvature Flows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35000)
*Jingwen Chen*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: The paper constructs counterexamples to Ilmanen's genus-reduction conjecture for closed mean curvature flows, by building flows whose forward rescaled limit is a prescribed expander asymptotic to a given conical singularity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Ilmanen's genus-reduction conjecture predicts that the genus decreases along singularities of mean curvature flow. The authors aim to disprove it by constructing closed flows whose tangent/expander behavior violates this expectation.

Method: They prove that a compact hypersurface with a single isolated conical singularity and power-rate convergence to its cone admits a mean curvature flow whose forward rescaled limit is a prescribed expander smoothly asymptotic to that cone. This is combined with previously established results on closed mean curvature flows with prescribed tangent flows and a noncompact counterexample.

Result: The theorem yields explicit counterexamples to Ilmanen's genus-reduction conjecture for closed mean curvature flows, obtained by combining the new expander-limit construction with prescribed-tangent-flow flows and the noncompact counterexample.

Conclusion: Ilmanen's genus-reduction conjecture fails for closed mean curvature flows; conical singularities can be evolved so that the forward rescaled limit is an arbitrary prescribed expander, producing genuine counterexamples.

Abstract: We construct counterexamples to Ilmanen's genus-reduction conjecture for closed mean curvature flows. We prove that a compact hypersurface with a single isolated conical singularity and a power rate of convergence to its cone admits a mean curvature flow whose forward rescaled limit is a prescribed expander smoothly asymptotic to that cone. Combining this theorem with closed mean curvature flows with prescribed tangent flows (arXiv:2405.15577, arXiv:2607.23879) and the noncompact counterexample (arXiv:2605.09657) gives the desired examples.

</details>


### [147] [Bach-flat metrics and global regularity of conformal structures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35257)
*Rodrigo Avalos,Albachiara Cogo,Andoni Royo Abrego*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: The paper establishes global regularity of conformal structures on compact manifolds with low-regularity (Sobolev) metrics, showing sharp conditions for conformal smoothness and solving related conformally invariant variational problems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conformally invariant variational problems often yield critical metrics with only Sobolev regularity; it is unclear when such low-regularity conformal structures are actually equivalent to smooth ones.

Method: Analysis of conformal structures on compact manifolds, deriving sharp Sobolev-regularity thresholds guaranteeing conformal diffeomorphism to a smooth metric; applied to Bach-flat four-manifolds and the Yamabe problem on three-manifolds.

Result: W^{2,q} Bach-flat four-manifolds with q>2 are smooth up to conformal diffeomorphisms; the Yamabe problem is solved for W^{2,q} metrics with q>2 on three-manifolds under a mild conformal condition.

Conclusion: Sharp Sobolev thresholds guarantee conformal smoothness, yielding regularity for Bach-flat four-manifolds and a solution to the Yamabe problem for low-regularity metrics on three-manifolds.

Abstract: We study global regularity properties of conformal structures on compact manifolds with applications to conformally invariant variational problems. We find sharp conditions ensuring that a manifold equipped with a Riemannian metric of Sobolev class is conformally diffeomorphic to a smooth one. In particular, we prove that $W^{2,q}$ Bach-flat four-manifolds with $q > 2$ are smooth up to conformal diffeomorphisms. We also solve the Yamabe problem for $W^{2,q}$ metrics with $q > 2$ on three-manifolds under a mild conformal condition.

</details>


### [148] [Determinants of Dirichlet and Neumann Laplacians on Constant Curvature Surfaces with Geodesic Polygonal Boundary](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35383)
*Victor Kalvin*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: The authors express zeta-regularized determinants of Dirichlet and Neumann Laplacians on compact constant-curvature Riemann surfaces with geodesic polygonal boundary in terms of the Laplacian determinant of their closed conical doubles, with no restriction on genus or number of boundary components.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Determinants of Laplacians on surfaces with boundary are hard to compute directly, and a relation to the better-understood determinants on closed doubled surfaces would allow boundary determinants to be obtained from known closed-surface formulae.

Method: They combine a relative BFK gluing formula with the Lück–Vishik comparison theorem for analytic and combinatorial torsion on manifolds with boundary. The resulting formula involves explicit corner angle factors and a quotient of the Reidemeister and L^2 norms on the determinant line of cohomology.

Result: Formulae relating boundary determinants to the determinants on conical doubles, valid for all genera and numbers of boundary components. As applications, a rigorous proof of the Aurell–Salomonson formula for Euclidean polygons, and explicit determinants for spherical and hyperbolic triangles and certain other geodesic polygons derived from known formulae for their doubles.

Conclusion: Boundary Laplacian determinants on geodesic polygonal surfaces are governed by their closed conical doubles up to explicit corner-angle and torsion-norm factors, yielding new explicit determinant formulae and a rigorous derivation of a previously formal polygon formula.

Abstract: We express the zeta regularized determinants of the Dirichlet and Neumann Laplacians on compact Riemann surfaces with constant curvature metrics and geodesic polygonal boundary in terms of the Laplacian determinant on their closed conical doubles. The formulae apply without restrictions on the genus or number of boundary components. They involve explicit corner angle factors and a quotient of the Reidemeister and $L^2$ norms on the determinant line of cohomology. The proof combines a relative BFK gluing formula with the comparison theorem for analytic and combinatorial torsion on manifolds with boundary, due to Lück and Vishik. As an application, we give a rigorous proof of the Aurell-Salomonson formula for Euclidean polygons. We also obtain explicit determinants for spherical and hyperbolic triangles and certain other geodesic polygons from known formulae for their doubles.

</details>


### [149] [The quermassintegral inequalities for k-convex spiral-star-shaped domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35394)
*Junfang Li*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: The paper extends quermassintegral inequalities from star-shaped domains to a broader class called "spiral-star-shaped" domains, defined via the positivity of ⟨X+AX, ν⟩ for a skew-symmetric map A, and proves long-time existence with exponential convergence to a round sphere for a locally constrained inverse curvature flow on k-convex spiral-star-shaped domains.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The earlier quermassintegral inequalities (Guan–Li 2009) were restricted to star-shaped domains, limiting their generality. The authors aim to enlarge the admissible class of domains while still obtaining sharp geometric inequalities and convergence to spheres.

Method: They introduce spiral-star-shaped domains characterized by ⟨X+AX, ν⟩ > 0 for a fixed skew-symmetric linear map A, where X+AX is a conformal Killing vector field mixing expansion (X) and rotation (AX). They study a locally constrained inverse curvature flow on smooth k-convex spiral-star-shaped domains, using the positive spiral support function for geometric control and monotonicity of quermassintegrals along the flow.

Result: For smooth k-convex spiral-star-shaped domains, the flow exists for all time and converges exponentially to a round sphere. Monotonicity of quermassintegrals along the flow yields sharp quermassintegral inequalities in this enlarged class, with equality if and only if the domain is a ball.

Conclusion: The results generalize the earlier quermassintegral inequalities from ordinary star-shaped domains to the strictly larger class of spiral-star-shaped domains (including non-star-shaped ones when A≠0), establishing sharp inequalities and stability of round spheres under the flow.

Abstract: We extend the quermassintegral inequalities of our earlier work \cite{GuanLi2009} from star-shaped domains to a substantially broader class of domains in Euclidean space, which we call spiral-star-shaped. A smooth domain is spiral-star-shaped if its boundary satisfies \[
  \langle X+A X,ν\rangle>0 \] for some fixed skew-symmetric linear map $ A$, where $X$ is the position vector and $ν$ is the outward unit normal. The vector field $X+A X$ is conformal Killing, with dilation component $X$ and rotational component $A X$; its trajectories combine expansion and rotation, giving rise to the spiral terminology. When $A=0$, it reduces to ordinary star-shapedness, while nonzero $A$ allows domains that are not star-shaped in the usual radial sense.
  For smooth $k$-convex spiral-star-shaped domains, we prove long-time existence and exponential convergence to a round sphere for a locally constrained inverse curvature flow. The positive spiral support function provides the geometric control needed to treat initial hypersurfaces that need not be star-shaped in the usual sense. Monotonicity of the quermassintegrals along the flow yields sharp quermassintegral inequalities in this enlarged class, with equality if and only if the domain is a ball.

</details>


### [150] [Index and nullity of critical spherical catenoids in hyperbolic three-space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35651)
*Ivan Dorofeev,Alexander Pigazzini*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: Every critical spherical catenoid in a geodesic ball of hyperbolic 3-space has Morse index 4 and nullity 2, resolving Medvedev's index conjecture and implying the supporting ball's radius strictly increases along the family.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Medvedev conjectured specific index/nulity values for critical spherical catenoids in hyperbolic 3-space geodesic balls, and this remained unproven across the full parameter range of the catenoid family.

Method: Combines a global boundary inequality derived from a Wronskian, a positive Dirichlet eigenfunction from the time component of the unit normal, and a Riccati comparison for the angular mode k=2, plus a ground-state identity to establish positivity of all angular modes k>=2.

Result: Each critical spherical catenoid has Morse index 4 and nullity 2; the kernel equals normal components of rotations preserving the ball's center and tilting the catenoid axis; the ball radius increases strictly along the family.

Conclusion: Medvedev's index conjecture is confirmed throughout the catenoid parameter range, providing an exact spectral characterization and a monotonicity result for the supporting ball radius.

Abstract: We prove that every critical spherical catenoid in a geodesic ball of hyperbolic three-space has Morse index four and nullity two. This resolves Medvedev's index conjecture throughout the parameter range of the catenoid family. The kernel consists precisely of the normal components of rotations that preserve the center of the ball and tilt the axis of the catenoid. As a consequence, the radius of the supporting ball increases strictly along the family. The proof combines a global boundary inequality obtained from a Wronskian, a positive Dirichlet eigenfunction supplied by the time component of the unit normal, and a Riccati comparison for the angular mode $k=2$. A ground-state identity then yields positivity of every angular mode $k\ge2$.

</details>


### [151] [Morse index characterization of the Veronese projective plane](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35820)
*Alessandro Carlotto,Yangyang Li,Zhihan Wang*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: This paper proves that the Veronese projective plane is the unique closed, connected, embedded minimal surface in S^4 (not totally geodesic) with Morse index exactly five, thereby settling Kusner's 1996 conjecture on Willmore minimizers in dimension four for surfaces with nonzero Euler number of the normal bundle.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Simons (1968) showed such surfaces have Morse index at least five, but it was unknown which surfaces attain this minimal index. This gap connects to Kusner's conjecture characterizing minimizers of the Willmore functional in dimension four.

Method: The authors combine their own analysis with recent advances by Bernstein and Ketover to characterize the equality case, using the connection between Morse index and Willmore minimizers, and exploiting the nonzero Euler number condition of the normal bundle.

Result: Equality (Morse index five) holds if and only if the surface is congruent to the Veronese projective plane. This characterizes the Willmore minimizers in dimension four for surfaces whose normal bundle has nonzero Euler number, including embeddings of nonorientable surfaces whose orientation double cover has even genus.

Conclusion: The Veronese projective plane is the unique index-five minimal surface in S^4, settling Kusner's conjecture under the nonzero Euler number hypothesis and answering which embedded minimal surfaces attain Simons' lower bound.

Abstract: Following Simons' seminal work in 1968, every closed, connected, embedded minimal surface in the four-sphere that is not totally geodesic has Morse index at least five. In this paper, we prove that equality holds if and only if the surface is congruent to the Veronese projective plane. Relying on recent advances by Bernstein and Ketover, this characterization settles Kusner's 1996 conjecture on the minimizers of the Willmore functional in dimension four for surfaces whose normal bundle has nonzero Euler number. This is always the case, in particular, for embeddings of nonorientable surfaces whose orientation double cover has even genus.

</details>


### [152] [Nonabelian Higgs fields from minimal submanifolds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35837)
*Marco Badran*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: The authors construct SU(2) Yang-Mills-Higgs fields on noncompact Riemannian manifolds that concentrate, in a large-mass limit and with uniformly bounded rescaled energy, along prescribed nondegenerate minimal submanifolds of codimension three; an analogous result holds on closed manifolds with b_1(M)=0 upon adding a scaled Higgs potential.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: A correspondence proposed by Parise, Pigati and Stern predicted that minimal submanifolds of codimension three arise as concentration sets of SU(2) Yang-Mills-Higgs fields; this paper aims to establish one direction of that proposal by actually building such concentrating fields.

Method: The construction proceeds on a broad class of noncompact Riemannian manifolds, working in the large-mass limit and using the prescribed nondegenerate minimal submanifold as the concentration set, with control of the rescaled energy; on closed manifolds with b_1(M)=0 a suitably scaled Higgs potential is added to obtain the analogous concentration behavior.

Result: The authors obtain SU(2) Yang-Mills-Higgs fields concentrating along any prescribed nondegenerate codimension-three minimal submanifold, with uniformly bounded rescaled energy, both on the noncompact setting and, with the added Higgs potential, on closed manifolds with b_1(M)=0.

Conclusion: The work completes one direction of the Parise-Pigati-Stern correspondence, realizing prescribed codimension-three minimal submanifolds as concentration sets of SU(2) Yang-Mills-Higgs fields; the reverse direction remains open.

Abstract: We construct $\mathrm{SU}(2)$ Yang-Mills-Higgs fields on a broad class of noncompact Riemannian manifolds, concentrating in the large-mass limit along prescribed nondegenerate minimal submanifolds of codimension three. The solutions have uniformly bounded rescaled energy. On closed manifolds with $b_1(M)=0$, adding a suitably scaled Higgs potential gives an analogous concentration result. Our construction completes one direction of the correspondence proposed by Parise, Pigati and Stern, realising prescribed minimal submanifolds of codimension three as concentration sets of $\mathrm{SU}(2)$ Yang--Mills--Higgs fields.

</details>


<div id='math.PR'></div>

# math.PR [[Back]](#toc)

### [153] [Persistence Pays: Square-root condition-number dependence for Metropolis-adjusted kinetic Langevin Monte Carlo with a non-reversibly updated accept/reject uniform](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35781)
*Nawaf Bou-Rabee*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: The paper proves accelerated mixing for a Metropolis-adjusted kinetic Langevin sampler (OBABO splitting with Neal's non-reversibly updated uniform), achieving total variation accuracy ε in Õ(√(κd) + √L_H (d/m)^{3/4}) gradient evaluations, with empirical confirmation on Bayesian logistic regression posteriors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Establish rigorous accelerated mixing guarantees for Metropolis-adjusted kinetic Langevin samplers, showing that the √κ dependence (necessary even on Gaussians) can be attained while controlling discretization error and energy errors.

Method: Discretizes Lu's entropic hypocoercivity via an entropy inequality for free transport, so the unadjusted chain's relative entropy decays at rate ~√m up to discretization bias. Neal's non-reversibly updated uniform makes leading velocity-Verlet energy errors telescope along a path; a coupling argument combines the two analyses.

Result: For targets e^{-U} with mI ⪯ ∇²U ⪯ LI and L_H-Lipschitz Hessian, the chain reaches TV accuracy ε after Õ(√(κd) + √L_H (d/m)^{3/4}) gradient evaluations (κ = L/m). The √κ term is necessary, already on Gaussian targets. On Bayesian logistic regression posteriors, gradient cost grows ~√κ for the non-reversibly updated uniform vs. ~κ^{3/4} for standard adjusted OBABO.

Conclusion: Accelerated mixing is proven for the adjusted OBABO sampler with the non-reversibly updated uniform, matching the necessary √κ lower bound up to logarithmic factors, and empirically outperforming standard adjusted OBABO on condition-number scaling.

Abstract: We prove accelerated mixing for a Metropolis-adjusted kinetic Langevin sampler: the OBABO splitting with friction $2\sqrt m$, adjusted with Neal's non-reversibly updated uniform, which is redrawn after every epoch of $E$ steps. For targets $e^{-U}$ on $\mathbb{R}^d$ with $mI\preceq\nabla^2U\preceq LI$ and an $L_H$-Lipschitz Hessian, started from a Gaussian at an approximate minimizer, the chain reaches total variation accuracy $\varepsilon$ after $\tilde O\big(\sqrt{κd}+\sqrt{L_H}(d/m)^{3/4}\big)$ gradient evaluations, where $κ=L/m$ and $\tilde O$ hides logarithmic factors. The $\sqrtκ$ term is necessary, already on Gaussian targets. The proof discretizes Lu's entropic hypocoercivity by means of an entropy inequality for free transport, so that the relative entropy of the unadjusted chain decays at a rate of order $\sqrt m$ up to a discretization bias. With the non-reversibly updated uniform, the leading energy errors of velocity Verlet telescope along a path. A coupling argument combines the two. On Bayesian logistic regression posteriors for two standard data sets, which satisfy these assumptions, the gradient cost of the chain with the non-reversibly updated uniform grows roughly like the square root of the condition number at the posterior mode, and that of standard adjusted OBABO roughly like its $3/4$ power.

</details>


### [154] [Operator limits of random matrices: Edge universality and sharp approximations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35808)
*Hyung-Gil Kwak*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: The paper proves operator universality at the spectral edge of general Wigner matrices by showing that rescaled Lanczos tridiagonalizations with deterministic initial vectors converge to the stochastic Airy operator with Dirichlet boundary condition, and constructs sharp couplings of Gaussian, Laguerre, and circular β-ensembles to stochastic Airy, Bessel, and Sine operators with explicit almost-sure error orders.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Edge universality for Wigner matrices is well known at the level of eigenvalues, but operator-level universality (the full tridiagonal Lanczos structure converging to a limiting differential operator) is harder because Lanczos coefficients for non-Gaussian Wigner matrices are strongly dependent. The goal is to establish spectral-edge operator universality without relying on Dyson Brownian motion in the Green function comparison step, and to obtain sharp couplings between classical β-ensembles and stochastic operators.

Method: The authors control the entire tridiagonal structure on the scale relevant to the limiting differential operator. They combine multi-resolvent local laws with quantitative control of Krylov subspaces, and compare the resulting Jacobi coefficients to those of Gaussian ensembles. This identifies the limiting Brownian potential directly from the original matrix entries, avoiding Dyson Brownian motion in the Green function comparison. For Gaussian, Laguerre, and circular β-ensembles, they construct sharp couplings to the stochastic Airy, Bessel, and Sine operators that preserve finite-matrix laws.

Result: The resolvent limit gives joint convergence of edge eigenvalues, embedded eigenfunctions, and spectral measures to the stochastic Airy operator. For fixed β>0, the squared Hilbert-Schmidt inverse errors of the couplings to Airy, Bessel, and Sine operators have almost-sure orders n^{-1/3}, n^{-1}, and n^{-1} respectively, with a>0 in the hard-edge upper bound. The positive leading-order coefficient for the circular ensemble is identified, and its second derivative jumps at β=2.

Conclusion: Operator universality at the spectral edge holds for general Wigner matrices, extending edge universality beyond eigenvalues to the full resolvent/Lanczos structure. Sharp couplings between classical β-ensembles and stochastic Airy, Bessel, and Sine operators are established with near-optimal almost-sure error rates, and a β=2 transition phenomenon in the circular ensemble's error coefficient is revealed.

Abstract: We establish operator universality at the spectral edge of general Wigner matrices by showing that their suitably rescaled Lanczos tridiagonalizations, constructed from deterministic initial vectors, converge to the stochastic Airy operator with Dirichlet boundary condition.
  The result requires control of the entire tridiagonal structure on the scale relevant to the limiting differential operator. The Lanczos coefficients for non-Gaussian Wigner matrices are strongly dependent. We analyze their fluctuations by combining multi-resolvent local laws with quantitative control of Krylov subspaces and a comparison of the resulting Jacobi coefficients with those of the Gaussian ensembles. This approach identifies the limiting Brownian potential directly from the original matrix entries, without invoking Dyson Brownian motion in the step of Green function comparison. The resolvent limit yields joint convergence of edge eigenvalues, embedded eigenfunctions, and spectral measures.
  For Gaussian, Laguerre and circular $β$-ensembles we construct sharp couplings to the stochastic Airy, Bessel and Sine operators, preserving the finite-matrix laws. For fixed $β> 0$, their squared Hilbert-Schmidt inverse errors have almost-sure orders $n^{-1/3}, n^{-1}$ and $n^{-1}$, respectively, with $a > 0$ in the hard-edge upper bound. We also identify the positive leading order coefficient for the circular ensemble, whose second derivative jumps at $β= 2$.

</details>


### [155] [Uniform Large Deviations, Barrier Identity and the Néel--Arrhenius Law for the One-Dimensional Stochastic Landau--Lifshitz--Gilbert Equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34823)
*Zdzisław Brzeźniak,Fan Gu,Liang Li*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: The paper analyzes the 1D stochastic Landau-Lifshitz-Gilbert (LLG) equation driven by regularised multiplicative degenerate trace-class noise and computes the thermally induced magnetisation reversal rate, showing an Arrhenius law with a delta-independent barrier.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Physicists model thermally induced magnetisation reversal via the stochastic LLG equation, but rigorous derivation of the exponential escape rate requires a large deviation theory and a tractable (non-degenerate, well-defined) noise; space-time white noise is analytically problematic, so one needs to handle regularised degenerate multiplicative noise and then remove the regularisation.

Method: (i) Establish a uniform large deviation principle on C([0,T];H) with a good rate function, uniformly over initial data in a V-bounded set with compact H-closure, without assuming noise non-degeneracy. (ii) Pass to the delta -> 0 limit: show the state-space quasi-potential U_delta converges in the Gamma-sense to U_0 of the limiting control problem (Q_0^{1/2}=I), proving the barrier inf over the boundary of D is delta-independent and equals c_* times the inf of the micromagnetic energy E on that boundary, c_* = 2 lambda_2/(lambda_1^2 + lambda_2^2), under two structural assumptions and no geometric/transversality input. (iii) Combine with the classical exit-time theorem.

Result: Uniform LDP holds at fixed delta; U_delta -> U_0 in the Gamma-sense though pointwise convergence fails (Gamma-limit is the sharp statement); the barrier is delta-independent and equals c_* times the infimum of E over the boundary of D. The escape time tau from D={E<r} satisfies both levels of the Neel-Arrhenius law: eps log E[tau] -> barrier uniformly on compacts for each fixed delta, and lim_{delta->0} lim_{eps->0} eps log E[tau] = c_* inf_{boundary D} E.

Conclusion: The Arrhenius exponent for thermally induced magnetisation reversal is stable under regularisation of the noise and is determined by the micromagnetic energy alone; the result requires only structural assumptions and holds without non-degeneracy or geometric/transversality conditions.

Abstract: We study the one-dimensional stochastic Landau-Lifshitz-Gilbert equation with a regularised, multiplicative, degenerate trace-class noise of strength sqrt(eps), the regularisation being Q_delta^{1/2} = (I + delta A^{beta/2})^{-1}, A = -d^2/dx^2, beta > 3/2, and determine the exponential rate of thermally induced magnetisation reversal.
  (i) Uniform large deviations at fixed delta: for every delta > 0 the solution satisfies a large deviation principle on C([0,T];H), with a good rate function, uniformly in the initial datum over a V-bounded set with compact H-closure; no non-degeneracy of the noise is assumed.
  (ii) Removal of the regularisation: as delta tends to 0 the noise tends to space-time white noise. With U_delta the state-space quasi-potential and U_0 that of the limiting control problem (Q_0^{1/2} = I), U_delta converges to U_0 in the Gamma-sense, and the barrier is delta-independent: the infimum of U_delta over the boundary of D equals that of U_0 and equals c_* times the infimum of the micromagnetic energy E over that boundary, c_* = 2 lambda_2/(lambda_1^2 + lambda_2^2), under two structural assumptions only and with no geometric or transversality input. Pointwise convergence fails, so the Gamma-limit is the sharp form of the statement.
  (iii) Neel-Arrhenius law: combining (i) and (ii) with the classical exit-time theorem, the escape time tau from D = {E < r} satisfies both levels of the law - for every fixed delta > 0, eps log E[tau] converges, uniformly on compact subsets of the initial set, to the barrier, and lim_{delta->0} lim_{eps->0} eps log E[tau] = c_* times the infimum of E over the boundary of D. The Arrhenius exponent is therefore stable under the regularisation of the noise and is determined by the micromagnetic energy alone.

</details>


<div id='math.OC'></div>

# math.OC [[Back]](#toc)

### [156] [ADACT: PDE-Constrained Thermal Hardware/Software Co-Design Adjoint-Driven Optimal Control via Finite Difference Discretization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35569)
*Don Li*

Main category: math.OC

TL;DR: ADACT is a hardware/software co-design, physics-informed PDE-constrained optimal control framework for dynamic thermal management in multi-core microprocessors, combining a Crank-Nicolson forward thermal solver with a continuous backward-in-time adjoint method and L-BFGS to jointly throttle power workloads and cooling while upholding thermal thresholds and throughput.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Large-scale AI and HPC workloads impose severe power density limits and thermal throttling on modern multi-core microprocessors, creating an urgent need for principled, predictive, physics-informed dynamical thermal management rather than ad hoc throttling.

Method: The temperature field is modeled by a 2D nonhomogeneous parabolic reaction-diffusion equation with localized time-varying power sources and variable convective cooling under nonhomogeneous Dirichlet boundary conditions. It is discretized with a Crank-Nicolson finite difference scheme and a 5-point Laplacian on a uniform mesh (unconditionally stable, second-order accurate in space and time). Exact gradients are obtained via a continuous backward-in-time adjoint state method, with L-BFGS as the optimizer; solvers are verified by the Method of Manufactured Solutions and gradient accuracy by directional derivative Taylor tests.

Result: Forward and adjoint solvers are quantitatively verified to achieve second-order convergence rates via MMS, and gradient accuracy is validated with Taylor tests. Numerical experiments show ADACT dynamically throttles software power workloads and modulates hardware cooling to strictly uphold physical thermal thresholds while maintaining operational throughput and minimizing thermal risk and cooling energy overhead.

Conclusion: ADACT offers a principled, verified physics-informed co-design optimal control approach that simultaneously balances thermal safety, energy efficiency, and execution throughput for thermal management of modern multi-core microprocessors.

Abstract: By virtue of the sheer compute demands of large-scale artificial intelligence (AI) and high-performance computing (HPC), modern multi-core microprocessors suffer from severe power density limits and thermal throttling, entailing an urgent need for principled methods for predictive, physics-informed dynamical thermal management (DTM). Here, we introduce a hardware/software co-design physics-informed PDE-constrained DTM throttling optimal control algorithm, ADACT (Adjoint-Driven Adaptive Co-Design Thermal Control), that minimizes thermal risk and cooling energy overhead while maximizing core execution throughput. The underlying physical temperature field is governed by a two-dimensional nonhomogeneous parabolic reaction-diffusion equation with spatially localized, time-varying power source terms and variable convective cooling subject to nonhomogeneous Dirichlet boundary conditions. We employ a discretization of the continuous state PDE via a Crank-Nicolson finite difference scheme with a 5-point discrete Laplacian operator on a uniform spatial mesh, which preserves unconditional numerical stability and $O(Δx^2 + Δy^2 + Δt^2)$ second-order temporal and spatial accuracy. To efficiently compute exact gradients, we construct and implement a continuous backward-in-time adjoint state method with L-BFGS as our optimization algorithm of choice. The accuracy and second-order convergence rates of both forward state and backward adjoint solvers are quantitatively verified via the Method of Manufactured Solutions (MMS), and gradient accuracy is validated using directional derivative Taylor tests. Numerical experiments demonstrate that ADACT dynamically throttles software power workloads and modulates hardware cooling efforts to strictly uphold physical thermal thresholds while maintaining operational throughput.

</details>


### [157] [Local Algebraic Stability of Wasserstein Gradient Descent-Ascent and Mean Field Langevin Dynamics without Spectral Gap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35528)
*Jing An,Lihan Wang*

Main category: math.OC

TL;DR: Wasserstein GDA for entropy-regularized zero-sum games on noncompact domains converges only algebraically (not exponentially) in chi-squared divergence when equilibrium measures lack a Poincaré inequality; matching lower bounds show the rate is worst-case algebraic.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Entropy-regularized two-player zero-sum games typically have equilibrium measures that may not satisfy a Poincaré inequality on noncompact domains. In that case the spectral-gap arguments used to prove local exponential convergence of Wasserstein gradient descent-ascent break down, leaving the convergence behavior in this regime uncharacterized.

Method: The authors analyze local stability of Wasserstein GDA in chi-squared divergence, assuming the initial relative density is a small L^2 perturbation with bounded algebraic moments. The proof relies on two ingredients: (i) a cancellation in a negative Sobolev energy estimate, and (ii) a bootstrap argument that keeps moments uniformly bounded in time. Lower bounds are obtained by constructing perturbations supported far in the tails of polynomial-tailed equilibria that persist over diffusive time scales.

Result: Wasserstein GDA converges algebraically to the mixed Nash equilibrium in chi-squared divergence when one or both equilibrium measures fail to satisfy a Poincaré inequality. For polynomial-tailed equilibria, the constructed far-tail perturbations yield algebraic lower bounds on the worst-case relaxation rate. Analogous local algebraic stability and lower bound results are also established for mean-field Langevin dynamics without a spectral gap.

Conclusion: Without a spectral gap, Wasserstein GDA (and mean-field Langevin dynamics) exhibits algebraic rather than exponential local convergence, and this rate is sharp in the worst case. The analysis extends beyond Poincaré-based frameworks to noncompact settings with polynomial-tailed equilibria.

Abstract: We study the local stability of Wasserstein gradient descent-ascent (GDA) for entropy regularized two-player zero-sum games on noncompact domains. When one or both equilibrium measures fail to satisfy a Poincaré inequality, the spectral gap arguments underlying local exponential convergence are unavailable. we show that Wasserstein GDA converges algebraically to the mixed Nash equilibrium in chi-squared divergence, provided the initial relative density is a small perturbation in $L^2$ with bounded algebraic moments. The proof has two main ingredients: a cancellation in a negative Sobolev energy estimate, and a bootstrap argument that keeps the moments bounded uniformly in time. For polynomial-tailed equilibria, we construct perturbations supported far out in the tails that survive over diffusive time scales, which gives algebraic lower bounds on the worst-case relaxation rate. We also establish analogous local algebraic stability and lower bound results for mean-field Langevin dynamics, when the equilibrium fails to satisfy a spectral gap.

</details>
